“ciṅkak kāṉmēl titta tūṅku kaṭṭiliṉ ilaṅkum māṭcimi viḷaṅkum aṉṉattūviyāṟ ceyta melliya paṭukki” eṉak kalit toki 13 il oru talivaṉiṉ paṭukkiyiṉ viparaṅ kāṇappaṭukiṉṟatu. neṭunalvāṭiyil yāṉik kompiṉāṟ ceyyappaṭṭatum, nāṭā viṉāṟ piṉṉappaṭṭatum palvēṟu mettikaḷ pōṭṭup pūkkaḷiṉitaḻ parappit tukiliṉ tūmaṭi virikkappaṭṭatumākiya kaṭṭil eṉṟum, kaṭṭi liṉ mēl vitāṉattil tiṅkaḷum urōkiṇiyum eḻutappaṭṭiruntaṉa veṉṟum kūṟappaṭukiṉṟatu.
“puli vaṭivaminta kāliyuṭiya kaṭṭilil elimayirāṟ ceyyap paṭṭa pūttoḻiliyuṭiya pōrviyi virittuk kappalilē vanta palavakiyāṉa paṭukkikaḷil ārāyntu paṭuttu” eṉap peruṅkatiyil kūṟappaṭukiṉṟatu. ivi celvarkaḷukkuriya paṭukkikaḷākum;
āyar taṭikaḷiṉāl varintu kaṭṭappaṭṭa kaṭṭilil āṭṭut tōlip pāyākak koṇṭu uṟaṅkuvārkaḷeṉṟum, eyiṟṟiyar māṉṟōlip paṭukkiyākak koḷvārkaḷeṉṟum ilakkiyattiṟ collappaṭukiṉṟaṉa.
5. viḷakkukaḷ
vaṟiṟōr maṇṇiṉāl ceyyappaṭṭa viḷakkukaḷip payaṉpaṭut tiṉar. ēṉiyōr irumpu cempu veṇkalam mutaliyavaṟṟāṟ ceyyap paṭṭa viḷakkukakiḷ payaṉpaṭuttiṉar. yavaṉarkaḷāṟ koṇṭuvarappaṭṭa aṉṉa viḷakku, pāvi viḷakku mutaliyaṉavum upayōkikkappaṭṭaṉa.
“yavaṉa rōtima viḷakku” (perumpāṇ)
“pāvi viḷakkiṟ parūuccuṭar aḻala” (mullippāṭṭu)
“yavaṉap pāvi aṇi viḷakku” (peruṅkati)
6. paṭṭaṅkaḷ
ikkālam aracarāl aḷikkappaṭum paṭṭaṅkaḷip pōl tamiḻ aracar pōr vīrarukkum piṟarukkum pala paṭṭaṅkaḷi aḷittaṉar. eṭṭippaṭṭam-itu eṭṭip pūvippōl poṉṉāl ceytu vaṇikarukkuk koṭukkum paṭṭam. ēṉātip paṭṭam-cēṉāpatiyar mutalāyiṉōrkku aracaṉāl aḷikkappaṭum paṭṭam. kāvitip paṭṭam-uḻuvittuṇpōrkaḷiṟ ciṟantōr aracaṉāṟ peṟum paṭṭam. ēṉātip paṭṭattukku mōtiramaḷittal vaḻakku. “ēṉāti mōtirañ ceṟikkun tiruvuṭiyāṉoruvaṉ” eṉpatu iṟiyaṉārakap poruḷuri.
7. veḷḷaṇi nāḷ
aracar tām piṟanta nāḷil veḷḷaṇi aṇintu koṇṭāṭuvar. avar muṭicūṭṭum nāḷiyum perunāḷākak koṇṭāṭutal marapu. innāḷil aracaṉ ciṟikkōṭṭattuḷḷōri viṭutali ceyvāṉ.
8. cila paḻakka vaḻakkaṅkaḷ
pāṇṭiyanāṭu muttukkuppērpōṉatu. ‘mārkō pōlō’ eṉpavar muttuk kuḷippip paṟṟik kūṟiyavaralāṟu uṇmiyāyirukkiṟatu. “cuntarapāṇṭi tēvaruṭiya nāṭṭil mutaṟṟaramāṉa periya muttukkaḷ kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. avi eppaṭi eṭukkappaṭukiṉṟaṉa eṉpatip paṟṟic collukiṟēṉ. muttukkuḷippōr peritum ciṟitumākiya marakkalaṅ kaḷik koṇṭu ilaṅkit tīvukkum intiyāvukkum iṭiyiluḷḷa kuṭāvukkuc celkiṟārkaḷ. avarkaḷ mutalil pettilār (Bettlar) eṉṉum ōr iṭatti aṭikiṟārkaḷ. piṉpu kuṭāvukkuḷ aṟupatu mil tūram celkiṉṟaṉar. iṅkē avarkaḷ tōṇikaḷi naṅkūramiṭṭuc ciṟiya vaḷḷaṅkaḷil ēṟikkoḷkiṉṟaṉar. aṅkē vaṇikarkaḷ pala kūṭṭaṅ kaḷākap pirintu celkiṉṟaṉar. avarkaḷ muttuk kuḷippavarkaḷic cittiri mātan toṭaṅki vikāci mātam varikkum campaḷattukku amarttikkoḷkiṉṟaṉar. aṅkuk kiṭikkum muttukkaḷil patti loṉṟi aracaṉuṭiya kaṭamiyākak koṭuttuviṭal vēṇṭum: muttuk kuḷippōrip periya mīṉkaḷ tuṉpañ ceyyātiruttaṟ poruṭṭu avaṟṟi mantirattāl kaṭṭukiṉṟavarkaḷukku irupatil oṉṟu koṭukka vēṇṭum. maccaṅkaḷi mantiraṅkaḷāṟ kaṭṭupavarkaḷ pirāmaṇarkaḷāvar. avarkaḷuṭiya mantira vali pakal nērattil māttiram palikkum. iravilē maccaṅkaḷ taṅkaḷ eṇṇappaṭi tiriyum poruṭṭu mantiratti aviḻttu viṭukiṟārkaḷ. muttukkuḷippōr ciṟiya vaḷḷaṅkaḷil ēṟiyavuṭaṉ nālu mutal paṉṉiraṇṭu pākat taṇṇīruḷ kutittuc cuḻiyōṭi aṭiyiṟ ceṉṟu mūccaṭakkakkūṭiya variyil taṅkukiṟārkaḷ. kīḻē kāṇappaṭum muttuc cippikaḷip poṟukki ariyilē kaṭṭiyirukkum vali pōṉṟa oru pikkuṭpōṭṭukkoṇṭu mēlē vantu maṟupaṭiyum kīḻē celkiṉṟārkaḷ. ivvakiyākap peruntokiyāṉa cippikaḷ eṭukkap paṭukiṉṟaṉa.1 iccippikaḷiliruntu ulakam muḻumikkum celkiṉṟa muttukkaḷ eṭukkappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. muttukkuḷippiṉāl aracaṉ perun tokiyāṉa varuvāyaṭikiṟāṉ. ari cākkiyō (Saggio) niṟikku mēṟpaṭṭa muttukkaḷi oruvarāvatu avaratu tēcattukkappāl koṇṭu pōtal kūṭātu. avvakiyāṉa muttukkaḷit tāṉ peṟa virumpiyē aracaṉ avvakiyāṉa caṭṭatti uṇṭākkiyirukkiṟāṉ. avaṉiṭatti luḷḷa muttukkaḷ eṇṇikkiyillātaṉa. periya muttukkaḷ allatu irattiṉaṅkaḷ vittiruppavarkaḷ avaṟṟit taṉṉiṭam koṇṭu vantāl atu peṟum viliyil iru maṭaṅkutāṉ koṭuppatāka āṇṭuk korumuṟi nāṭu muḻumiyum aṟivikkiṟāṉ. avvakiyāṉa muttukkaḷ irattiṉaṅkaḷ vittiruppōr avaṟṟi aracaṉiṭattil koṭuttu viliyip peṟṟukkoḷkiṉṟaṉar.
muttukkaḷit tavira vēṟu viliyuyarnta poruḷkaḷiyum aracaṉ vittirukkiṟāṉ. avaṉ palavaki irattiṉaṅkaḷ patitta āraṅ kaḷik kaḻuttil aṇintirukkiṟāṉ. 104 periya muttukkaḷum irattiṉaṅ kaḷum cērkkappaṭṭa oru paṭṭunūl avaṉ mārpilē toṅkukiṉṟatu. avaṉ kāliyilum māliyilum 104 tutikaḷik kaṭavuḷukkuc colla vēṇṭiyiruppatāl iṅṅaṉam aṇintirukkiṟāṉeṉṟu collappaṭu kiṉṟatu. ivvakiyākavē ivaṉ muṉṉōrum ceytu vantārkaḷ. muṉṉōr vittirunta ārattiyē ivaṉ peṟṟāṉ.
muttukkaḷ aḻuttic ceyyappaṭṭa mūṉṟu poṉ vaḷiyalkaḷi aracaṉ tōḷilē pūṇṭirukkiṟāṉ. avi pōṉṟa vaḷiyalkaḷik kālilum aṇintirukkiṉṟāṉ. kālviralil avi pōṉṟa mōtiraṅ kaḷiyumaṇintirukkiṉṟāṉ.
iṅṅaṉam aracaṉ aṇintirukkum aṇikaḷiṉ vili oru nāṭṭiṉ varuvāyilum atikamākum. ivvakiyāṉa poruḷkaḷ avaṉiṭattiṟ pala uṇṭu.
avaṉiṟakkumpōtu avaṉuṭiya piḷḷikaḷ kaḷañciyattit tīṇṭamāṭṭārkaḷ. ‘eṅkaḷ tanti evvaḷavu cēmittu vittiruk kiṟārō avvaḷavu nāṅkaḷum tēṭi vittarukka vēṇṭu’ meṉac collukiṟārkaḷ. vāciv (Wassif) eṉṉum makamatiya caritāciriyar kūṟuvati liruntu mārkkō pōlō pāṇṭiyarkaḷ tiraṭṭi vittirunta poruḷ niliyic cariyāka aṟintiruntāṉeṉpatu viḷaṅkukiṉṟatu. “kulacēkara ṉatu karuvūlam vilimatippuḷḷa poruḷ niṟintatu. avaṉatu karuvūlam 12,000 kōṭi uḷḷatāyiruntatu. itiviṭa muttu pavaḻam irattiṉam mutaliya pala poruḷkaḷ iruntaṉa. avaṉuṭiya celvatti aḷaviṭṭuk kūṟa moḻi iṭaṅkoṭātu” eṉa avaṉ kūṟiyuḷḷār. mārkkōpōlō, aracaṉiyum avaṉatu capiyiyuṅkuṟittuk kēṭpataṟkiṉpamākiya ceytikaḷ kūṟukiṉṟāṉ. “inta aracaṉ aiññūṟu maṉiviyari uṭiyava ṉāyirukkiṟāṉ. avaṉukkup pala piḷḷikaḷuṇṭu. aracaṉic cūḻntu pala pirapukkaḷ irukkiṟārkaḷ. avarkaḷ avaṉuṭaṉkūṭac cavāriceyki ṟārkaḷ. avarkaḷ eppoḻutum aracaṉukkup pakkattil irukkiṟārkaḷ. aracāṅkattil avarkaḷukkup periya atikāramuṇṭu. avarkaḷ ‘āpat tukku utavikaḷ’ eṉṟu aḻikkappaṭuvārkaḷ. aracaṉ iṟantu eriyil koḷuttappaṭumpōtu av vāpattukkutavikaḷum pirētattic cūḻntu tīyil viḻuntu iṟantu pōkiṉṟārkaḷ. immiyil aracaṉukkutaviyā yirunta tām maṟumiyilum avaṉukku utaviyāyirukka vēṇṭumeṉṟu avarkaḷ collik koḷkiṉṟaṉar.
mārkkōpōlō potumakkaḷiṉ vāḻkkiyik kuṟittuk kūṟiyirukkiṉṟār. “avarkaḷuṭiya naṭi uṭi mutaliyaṉa avaṉi āccariyamuṟac ceytaṉa. ‘māpār’1 eṉṉum mākāṇattiluḷḷavarkaḷ arikku mēl niruvāṇikaḷākavēyiruntaṉar. ataṉāl aṅkē tiyaṟkārarkaḷ kāṇappaṭavilli. aracaṉ mutal vaṟiyavarkaḷ īṟāka ellōrum ariyil ōr āṭimāttiram tarittukkoḷvar.”
akkālattil (cati) uṭaṉkaṭṭi ēṟum vaḻakkam iruntateṉṟum kolikkut tīrkkappaṭṭa kuṟṟavāḷikaḷ tamatu viruppattiṉpaṭi yātēṉumoru kōyilukkut tammip paliyākak koṭuttuviṭalā meṉṟa piramāṇamiruntateṉṟum, caṉaṅkaḷ pacukkaḷi vaṇaṅkiṉārka ḷeṉṟum, māṭṭu māmicam akkālattiṟ pucikkappaṭavilliyeṉṟum mārkkōpōlō kūṟukiṉṟāṉ.
innāṭṭilē uḷḷavarkaḷ vīṭukaḷic cāṇiyāl meḻuku vārkaḷ. periyavarkaḷum ciṟiyavarkaḷumākiya ellōrum tariyilēyē iruppārkaḷ.
innāṭṭavar uṭampi maṟittuk koḷḷātu caṇṭikkuc celkiṉṟaṉar. avarkaḷ īṭṭiyum kēṭayamum vittiruppārkaḷ.
āṇkaḷum peṇkaḷum nāḷum irumuṟi kuḷippārkaḷ. appaṭic ceyyātavarkaḷ tāḻntavarkaḷākak karutappaṭṭārkaḷ. avarkaḷ valakkiyāl uṇavi eṭuttu uṇpatēyaṉṟi, iṭakkiyāl tīṇṭamāṭṭārkaḷ. ivvāṟē nīr parukukiṟārkaḷ. ovvoruvarum taṉittaṉi ovvoru pāttiram vittirukkiṉṟaṉar. avarkaḷ nīr aruntumpōtu pāttiram utaṭṭil muṭṭuvatilli. ekkāraṇattiṉā lāvatu avarkaḷ nīraruntum pāttiratti utaṭṭil muṭṭaviṭa māṭṭārkaḷ. aṉṉiyaṉoruvaṉ nīraruntap pāttiram vittirā viṭṭāl avaṉ uṇṇumpaṭi nīrik kiyil ūṟṟuvārkaḷ.
avarkaḷ kuṟṟavāḷikaḷukkut taṇṭaṉi iṟuppatil mikavum kavaṉam celuttiṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷ matu uṇpatilli. matupāṉañ ceypavarkaḷ nampikki nāṇayam uṭiyavarākak karutappaṭamāṭ ṭārkaḷ eṉṟu caṭṭañ ceytiruntārkaḷ. kaṭaṉ koṭuttavaṉ kaṭaṉ kāraṉip palamuṟiyum paṇattik kēṭka. avaṉ koṭāviṭṭāl, allatu pala keṭukkaḷ coṉṉāl avaṉ oru vaṭṭaṅ kīṟik kaṭaṉ kāraṉi ataṟkuḷ niṟuttalām. kaṭaṉ paṭṭavaṉ kaṭaṉ koṭuttavaṉit tiruptip paṭuttiyapiṉ allatu avaṉukkut takunta poṟuppuc ceyta piṉtāṉ avvaṭṭattiṉiṉṟum vilakalām. avaṉ ivvāṟu ceyyātu vaṭṭattiṉ veḷiyē celvāṉāyiṉ, avaṉukkuk kolit taṇṭaṉi vitikkappaṭukiṉṟatu.
avarkaḷiṉ cāmuttirikkā ilaṭcaṇam aṟintavarkaḷ palar uḷar. cakuṉam pārppatil avarkaḷukku mikunta nampikki. piḷḷi piṟantavuṭaṉ atu piṟanta mātam tēti nāḻiki mutaliyavaṟṟi eḻutivikkiṟārkaḷ. avarkaḷ ceyyum karumam ovvoṉṟum cōtiṭa campantamā yiruppatālēyē iṅṅaṉam ceykiṟārkaḷ.
“inta nakarattiluḷḷa caṉaṅkaḷum intiyāviṉ maṟu iṭaṅkaḷi luḷḷavarkaḷum ‘tāmpūlam’ eṉṉum oru vaki iliyi eppoḻutum vāykkuḷ vittu mellukiṉṟārkaḷ. avarkaḷ avviliyi meṉṟu umiḻ nīri veḷiyē tuppiviṭukiṉṟaṉar. aracarkaḷum celvarkaḷum karuppūram mutaliyaṉa kalanta vācaṉip poruḷkaḷuṭaṉ ataṉip payaṉpaṭuttukiṟārkaḷ. ivvaḻakkam nalattukkum mikavum nallateṉac collappaṭukiṟatu.
iṅkuk kutirikaḷ kiṭippatilli. itaṉāl piṟa nāṭukaḷiliruntu kutirikaḷ vāṅkuvatil innāṭu peruntokip paṇattic cela viṭṭatu. kis (Kis), hōms (Hormes), ṭōvar (Dofar), cōēr (Soer), eṭiṉ (Aden) mutaliya nāṭṭavarkaḷ inta nāṭṭu aracaṉukkum ava ṉuṭiya nālu cakōtararukkum viṟkumpaṭi kutirikaḷ koṇṭu varu vārkaḷ. oru kutiriyiṉ vili 500 cakki (Saggi) poṉṉākum. atu 100 veḷḷi mārks (Marks)kkuc cari.
‘vāciv’piṉ varumāṟu kūṟukiṟār:
kutirikaḷ iṅku vantatum paccivāḷi koṭuppataṟkup patil vaṟutta vāḷiyiyum, tāṉiyaṅkaḷiyum neyyuṭaṉ kalantu koṭuk kiṟārkaḷ. kuṭippataṟkuk kāycciya pacuviṉ pālik koṭukkiṟārkaḷ. avarkaḷ 40 nāṭkaḷukku avikaḷik kayiṟukaḷāl pantiyiṟ kaṭṭik koḻukkumpaṭi viṭukiṟārkaḷ. intiyap pōrvīrar avaṟṟiṉmītu tuṭṭa tēvatikaḷip pōla ēṟic cavāri ceykiṟārkaḷ. ciṟitu kālattukkup piṉ vēkamuḷḷa kutirikaḷ palamaṟṟaṉavāy upayōkamaṟṟup pōkiṉṟaṉa. itaṉāl āṇṭutōṟum putiya kutirikaḷ vāṅka nērkiṉṟatu.
mārkkōpōlō ki.pi. 1254 mutal 1324 variyil vāḻntavaṉ.
perumpāṇāṟṟuppaṭi
(itil paḻantamiḻariṉ paḻakka vaḻakkaṅkaḷ naṉku kūṟappaṭṭiruk kiṉṟaṉa)
iḷantiriyaṉpāṟ ceṉṟu paricil peṟṟāṉoruvaṉ. paricil peṟutaṟku virumpiya perumpāṇaṉoruvaṉi etirppaṭṭu avvaracaṉ pāl ēkumāṟu avaṉi āṟṟuppaṭutti iḷantiriyaṉiṉ irācatāṉi yākiya kāñcipurattiṟkuc cellum mārkkattiṉik kūṟukiṉṟāṉ.
“avaṉuṭiya nakaril vaḻippōkkar alaṟumpaṭi veṭṭi avar poruḷkaḷik koḷḷi koḷḷum tīyōrilli. avaṉuṭiya kāṭṭil iṭiyēṟum iṭiyātu; pāmpukaḷum tīṇṭā; puli mutaliya vilaṅkukaḷum tīṅkiḻiyā, avvakiyāṉa kāṭṭakattē nī payamiṉṟi iḷippāṟic cellalām. akkāṭṭil kavarpaṭṭa pala vaḻikaḷ uṇṭu. avvaḻikaḷil umaṇar uppēṟṟiya vaṇṭikaḷi ōṭṭic celvar. vaṇṭiyiṉ accu mattaḷam pōṉṟatu. ataṉ irumuṉikaḷilum cillukaḷ māṭṭap peṟṟirukkum. acciṉ ūṭē ēṇikkālkaḷ pōṉṟa iraṇṭu maraṅkaḷ māṭṭappeṟṟirukkum. ammaraṅkaḷil nāṭṭappaṭṭa taṭikaḷiṉ mēl kuṭilpōl pāyiṉāl vēyappaṭṭirukkum. vaṇṭikaḷiṉ muṉpuṟattē kōḻikkūṭukaḷ vikkappaṭṭirukkum. ural pōṉṟa uṟikaḷ vaṇṭikaḷiṉ muṉ puṟattē uyarak kaṭṭappaṭṭu avi mītu nellikkāy, puḷiyaṅkāy mutaliyaṉa ūṟaviṭṭa pāṉikaḷ vikkappaṭṭirukkum.
umaṇappeṇ kiyilē vēppiliyiyum piḷḷiyiyum uṭiyavaḷāy vaṇṭiyiṉ muṉpuṟattēyiruntu eruti mutukilē aṭit tōṭṭuvāḷ. taḻiyiṉāṟ kaṭṭiya māliyi aṇinta umaṇar, pala ciṟu tuḷikaḷiyuṭiya nukattilē kaṭṭappaṭṭa erutukaḷ vaṇṭiyit tirukāmalum accu muṟiyāmalum pakkattē kāttuk koṇṭum kaḷittāṟ pūṭṭuvataṟkup pala erutukaḷi ōṭṭikkoṇṭum uppiṉ viliyik kūṟikkoṇṭum kavarpaṭṭa vaḻikaḷāṟ celvar.
piṟanāṭukaḷiṉiṉṟum vanta vaṇikar māṇikkam, muttu, cantaṉam mutaliyavaṟṟi eṅkum tirintu viṟpārkaḷ. avarkaḷuṭiya mārpukaḷ ampukaḷāṟ kiḻikkappaṭṭu āṟiya taḻumpukaḷuṭiyaṉa. tammi etirppōrit tākkuvataṟkuk kiyilē vēl piṭittiruppar; yāṉit tantattāl kaṭinteṭutta uṟiyilē iṭṭavāḷi varikaḷiyuṭiya cīliyilē kōttut tōḷiṉ oru pakkattē toṅkaviṭṭuc caṭṭi yiṭṭuc cerupputtoṭṭu otta kaṉamākac cērnta miḷakup potikaḷik kavarpaṭṭa vaḻikaḷil cumantu cellum kaḻutikaḷiṉ piṉ celvārkaḷ. ivvāṟu cakaṭaṅkaḷum, kaḻutikaḷum celkiṉṟa kavarpaṭṭa vaḻikaḷ cantikkiṉṟa iṭattil aracaṉāl niyamikkappaṭṭa cuṅkaṅ koḷvōr viṟpaṭiyuṭaṉ kāttiruppārkaḷ.
akkāṭṭilē eyiṉarkaḷuṭiya ciṟu kuṭicikaḷ kāṇappaṭu kiṉṟaṉa. avi aṇilum eliyum nuḻiyātapaṭi īntiṉ ōliyiṉāl vēyappaṭṭu muṭpaṉṟiyiṉ mutukupōṉṟa puṟattiṉiyuṭiyaṉavā yirukkum.
piḷḷiyip peṟṟa eyiṟṟiyar akkuṭicikaḷiṉ orupuṟattē māṉṟōlkaḷil muṭaṅkik kiṭakka, maṟṟavarkaḷ pāriyiṉāl karampi nilattik kiḷaṟi, eṟumpu cērttu vitta pullariciyi eṭuttuvantu 1pārvimāṉ kaṭṭappaṭṭa viḷaviṉ niḻaliyuṭiya muṟṟattilē tōṇṭappaṭṭa nila uralilē peytu vayira ulakkiyiṉāl kuṟṟi, āḻnta kiṇaṟṟil uvar nīri moṇṭu vantu, paḻiya oṟuvāyp pōṉa pāṉiyilē uliyi vārttu, muṟinta aṭuppilē vittu ākku vārkaḷ. avar kuṭicikaḷi aṭintāl ivvāṟu camikkappaṭṭa cōṟṟi uppuk kaṇṭattuṭaṉē tēkkiliyil iṭṭu uṇṇum paṭi koṭuppārkaḷ.
ikkuṭicikaḷik kaṭantu ceṉṟāl eyiṉar araṇik kāṇa lām. aṅku matilkaḷiyuṭiyaṉavum ūkampulliṉāl vēyappaṭṭaṉavu mākiya vīṭukaḷ uṇṭu. avaṟṟuḷ pakivarik kuttiyatāl kūr maḻuṅkiya vēlkaḷ maṇikaṭṭiṉa paricikaḷōṭu viṟkaḷiṟ cāttappeṟṟirukkum talivāyiliṉ tiraṇṭa kālkaḷil ampuk kaṭṭukaḷum tuṭiyum tūṅkum. caṅkiliyilē kaṭṭappaṭṭa nāykaḷ vīṭṭik kāvalkāttu niṟkum. vīṭṭic cuṟṟi muḷvēliyum ataṉic cūḻntu kāvaṟkāṭum kāṇap paṭum. vāyiliṉ uṭkatavukaḷ tiraṇṭa maraṅkaḷāl tāḷ pōṭappaṭ ṭirukkum. ivvakiyāṉa eyiṉar araṇkaḷil taṅkiṉāl, nāykaḷ kaṭittuk koṇṭuvanta caṅkumaṇi pōṉṟa muṭṭikaḷiyuṭiya uṭumpiṉ poriyalālē maṟikkappaṭṭa cōṟṟi vīṭukaṭōṟum peṟuvāy.
appāṟ ceṉṟāl kuṟiñci nilatti aṭivāy. aṅkē vāṭṭoḻilic ceyyum kuṭiyiṟ piṟanta puliyiṉ mīciyip pōṉṟa tāṭiyiyuṭiya kuṟavar talivaṉ. koṭiya villiyuṭiya kāvala ruṭaṉē kāvaliyuṭiya pakivaratu mullinilatti aṭivāṉ. viṭiyaṟ kāliyilē avarkaḷ pacukkaḷi ōṭṭikkoṇṭupōyk kaḷḷukku viliyākak koṭuppāṉ. piṉ taṉatu vīṭṭilē nellāṟ ceyta kaḷḷi uṇṭu, āṭṭuk kiṭāyi muṟṟattil aṟuttu uṇṭu, mattaḷaṅ koṭṭa naṭuvē niṉṟu, iṭattōḷi valappakkattē vaḷittuk koṇṭu pakaṟpoḻutilē makiḻntu āṭuvāṉ. ivvakiyiṉatākiya kuṟiñci nilattik kaṭantapiṉpu mulli nilatti aṭivīrkaḷ.
aṅkē vīṭukaḷ kuṟukiya kālkaḷiṟ kaṭṭappaṭṭaṉa. akkuṟukiya kālkaḷil āṭṭumantikaḷ tiṉpataṟkut taḻikaḷ kaṭṭappaṭṭirukkum. vīṭṭiṉ vāyilkaḷil ciṟiya paṟṟikaḷ uṇṭu. katavukaḷ kayiṟṟiṉāl variyappaṭṭaṉa. kayiṟṟiṉāl variyappaṭṭu varaku vikkōl parappappaṭṭa paṭukkiyil kiṭāyiṉ tōlip paṭukkiyākavuṭiya mutiyōṉ kāvalākat tuyilum pala kuṭicikaḷ aṅkuk kāṇappaṭum. muṟṟattilē aṟiyappaṭṭirukkiṉṟa muḷikaḷil tāmaṇikaḷiyuṭiya kayiṟukaḷ kaṭṭappaṭṭirukkum. cīḻkki aṭikkiyiṉālē iṭiyar maṭitta vāyiyuṭiyavarkaḷāyiruppārkaḷ.
viṭiyaṟkālattilē māmi niṟattiyum tāḷuruvi acikiṉṟa kātiṉiyumuṭiya āymakaḷ mattu āravārikkumpaṭi tayirik kaṭintu veṇṇeyi eṭuppāḷ. piṉpu puḷḷiyākat tayirteṟitta mōrp pāṉiyip pūvāṟ ceyta cummāṭṭiṉmēl vittu mōri viṟpāḷ; viṟṟatiṉāṟ kiṭitta nel mutaliyavaṟṟik koṇṭupōyt taṉ cuṟṟattāri uṇṇappaṇṇuvāḷ; avaḷ tāṉ neyyi viṟṟa vilikkuk kaṭṭiyākiya pacumpoṉṉi vāṅkāḷāyp pālerumiyi yum, nalla pacuviṉiyum, eruminākiṉiyum koḷḷuvāḷ; iv vaki yiṉatākiya āyarkuṭiyiruppukaḷil taṅkuvāyāyiṉ, tiṉiyariciyālaṭṭa cilutta cōṟṟip pāluṭaṉē peṟuvāy.
appāl mulli nilattu ūrkaḷi aṭivīrkaḷ. aṅku vīṭu kaḷiṉ muṟṟattil varaku mutaliyaṉa pōṭṭu vikkum kūṭukaḷ (kutir) kāṇappaṭum. varaku tirikikaḷ talivāyilil naṭappaṭṭirukkum. koṭṭil vīṭukaḷ varaku vikkōliṉāl vēyappaṭṭirukkum. av vīṭukaḷiṉ oru puṟam aṭupperittatiṉāl puki cūḻntirukkum. vīṭukaḷiṉ cuvarkaḷil vaṇṭic cakkaraṅkaḷum kalappikaḷum cārtti vikkappaṭ ṭirukkum. aṅkē taṅkiṉāl varaku cōṟṟip peṟuvāy.
mulli nilattic cārnta marutattil vīṭu niṟinta uṇaviṉi yuṭiya uḻavar vayalkaḷi vitippārkaḷ. aṟukkum paruvattil ataṉ kaṇ taṅkum kuṟumpūḻ (kāṭi) paṟakkalāṟṟāta iḷiya piḷḷikaḷik kūṭṭik koṇṭu mulli nilattē cellum. vayalkaḷiluḷḷa tēṉāṟu kiṉṟa pūvi māliyākak kaṭṭic cūṭi, ataṉi veṟutta toḻil cey vāruṭiya piḷḷikaḷ muḷḷiyiṉ kariya pūvip paṟittuk kōriyip pallālē meṉṟu kiḻitta nārāṟ kaṭṭiya māliyi īruṭiya taliyilē yaṇintu kaṭampiṉ tāti mārpilē appuvārkaḷ. avarkaḷ paḻañcōṟṟi veṟuttup paṉṟi mutaliya kāttaṟku varampilē kaṭṭap paṭṭa putiya vikkōlāl vēynta ciṟiya kuṭiliṉ muṟṟattē avaliṭip pārkaḷ. avaliṭikkum ulakkiyiṉōci kiḷḷikaḷi veruvac ceyyum. vaḷinta katiriṉiyuṭiya vayaliṉiṭattu viḷinta nelli yaṟuppōr cilantiyiṉatu nūlkaḷ pakkattē cūḻnta pōrkaḷiṉ aṭiyi eṭuttu virippārkaḷ. piṉ ēr kaṭāviṭuvārkaḷ. vikkōliyum kūḷattiyum ataṉiṉiṉṟum nīkki mēṟkāṟṟilē tūvit tūṟṟiyaṉa poli malipōlat tōṉṟum. ivvakiyāṉa marutanilañ cērnta kuṭiyiruppukkaḷil vīṭṭiṉ pakkattē taṟikaḷil neṭiya kayiṟukaḷāl kaṉṟukaḷ kaṭṭappaṭṭu niṟkum. ēṇi eṭṭāta uyarattiyuṭiyatum tali tiṟantu uḷḷē coriyappaṭṭa paḻiya nelliṉiyuṭiyatumākiya tāṉiyak kūṭukaḷ vīṭukaḷil uṇṭu. taccacciṟār ceyta ciṟu tērkaḷip piḷḷikaḷ uruṭṭic ceṉṟa kaḷippālē cevilittāyaruṭiya pāli niṟiya uṇṭu paṭukkiyilē tuyilvārkaḷ. ivvakiyāṉa vaṟumi teriyāta kuṭiyiruppiṉiyuṭiya ūrilē nalla neṟcōṟṟiṉik kōḻic cēvaliṉ camitta poriyalōṭē peṟuvāy.
aṅkē puki cūḻnta koṭṭilkaḷil karuppañcāṟṟik kāyccuvārkaḷ. ak koṭṭilkaḷi aṭintāl muṉ karuppañ cāṟṟik koṭuttup piṉkaṭṭiyi uṇṇumpaṭi taruvārkaḷ.
appāṟ ceṉṟāl, valiñar kuṭiyiruppi aṭivīrkaḷ. avarkaḷ vīṭukaḷ kāñci vañci mutaliya maraṅkaḷiṉ kompukaḷik kikaḷukku naṭuvē tūṇāka naṭṭu, mūṅkil taṭikaḷi variccāka niṟittut tāḻi nārāṟ kaṭṭit taruppip pullāl vēynta tāḻnta iṟappi yuṭiyaṉavākum. mīṉkaḷi vāri eṭukkum valikaḷ muṟṟattilē kāṇappaṭum. talivāyiliṉ kūriyiṟ paṭarntuḷḷa curikkoṭiyil kāykaḷ toṅkum. iḷiyavarkaḷum mutiyavarkaḷum talivāyiliṭattil kūṭiyiruntu, piṉ iṟālum kayalum piṟaḻum āḻnta kuḷaṅkaḷil piḷḷikaḷōṭē ulāvi mīṉkaḷip piṭippārkaḷ. avarkaḷ kuṭiyirup pil taṅkiṉāl, kuṟṟāta koḻiyalariciyik kaḷiyākat tuḻāviyaṭṭa kūḻi, akaṉṟa vāyiyuṭiya taṭṭuppiḻāvilē ularavāṟṟip pāmpu kiṭakkiṉṟa puṟṟām paḻañcōṟṟiṉiyokkum puṟattiṉiyuṭiya muḷiyiṉi yiṭittuc cēra atilē kalantu cāṭiyiṉkaṇṇē muṟṟiya tum, viralālē alittu arikkun taṉmiyiyuṭiyatumākiya vevviya kaḷḷi mīṉcūṭṭuṭaṉ peṟuvāy. nīrttuṟiyilē viḷiyāṭi makaḷir pōkaṭṭuppōṉa kuḻiyiṉi nīlaniṟamuḷḷa ciccilippaṟavi taṉakku iriyeṉa eṭuttukkoṇṭu paṭcikaḷ niṟintirukkum paṉiyiṟ pōkātu antaṇar yākacāliyil naṭṭa yūpattiṉmēlirukkum. atu cōṉakar pāymarattiṉ mēlēṟṟiya aṉṉaviḷakkip pōlavum viṭiveḷḷi pōlavum oḷi viṭṭut tōṉṟum.
kaṭaṟkari, mēṟṟicikkaṇṇuḷḷa kutirikaḷiyum vaṭaticik kaṇṇuḷḷa poruḷkaḷiyum koṇṭu vantu tarum marakkalaṅkaḷ cūḻappeṟṟatu. maṇal mikunta terukkaḷil toḻilāḷarāṟ kākkappaṭum paṇṭakacālikaḷum paratavar vāḻum vāṉaḷāviya māḷikikaḷum uṇṭu. vīṭukaḷil viḷiporuḷkaḷ tēṅkik kiṭakkum. uḻukiṉṟa erutu kaḷuṭaṉ pacukkaḷ neruṅkātapaṭi āṭṭukkiṭāykaḷum nāykaḷum cuḻaṉṟu tiriyum. uyarnta māṭattuṟiyum pēraṇikalaṉkaḷiyuṭiya makaḷir paciya maṇikaḷ kōtta vaṭaṅkaḷiyuṭiya alkuliṉiṭattē kiṭakkiṉṟa melliya tukilaciya, maliyilē āravārikkiṉṟa tōkimayil pōl ulāvuvārkaḷ; kāliṭattēyuḷḷa poṟcilampukaḷ olikka, nūliṉāl varintu ceyyappaṭṭa pantiṉi aṭittu viḷiyāṭuvārkaḷ. mutti yotta vārnta maṇalil metteṉak kiyiṟṟaritta vaḷikaḷ aciyum paṭiyākap poṟkaḻaṅku koṇṭu viḷiyāṭuvārkaḷ.
kaḷḷuṇpār palarum pukukiṉṟa vāyilil paciya koṭikaḷ aciyum paṭi kaṭṭappaṭṭuḷḷaṉa. muṟṟattilē teyvattukkut tūviṉa civanta pūvāṭalkaḷ kiṭakkum. aṅkē kaḷḷic camikkiṉṟa makaḷir vaṭṭil kaḻuviya nīr vaṭitaliṉ nilañ cēṟāyirukkum. accēṟṟil puraḷukiṉṟa peṇ paṉṟiyuṭaṉē puṇarccik karuttāla pōkāmal kuḻiyilē niṟutti, nelli iṭitta māvākiya uṇaviṉāl āṇ paṉṟi vaḷarkkappaṭukiṟatu. ivvakiyāṉa paṭṭiṉattil taṅkuvāyāyiṉ appaṉṟiyiṉ taciyōṭu kaḷḷi uṇṇumpaṭi peṟuvāy. tuṟimukattiṉ kaṇṇē ākāyatti muṭṭumpaṭi uyarntatum kaṟṟi mutaliyavaṟṟāl vēyātatumākiya kalaṅ kari viḷakkam uṇṭu. itu marakkalaṅkaḷ tām cērum iṭatti yaṟintu cellumpaṭi niṟuvappaṭṭatu. itaṉik kaṭantu ceṉṟāl teṉṉaṅkīṟṟu kaḷāl vēynta vīṭukaḷ kāṇappaṭum. ivvakiyāṉa vīṭukaḷilē taṅkiṉāl palāppaḻattiyum, iḷanīriyum vāḻippaḻattiyum paṉi yiṉatu nuṅkōṭē vēṟu paṇṭaṅkaḷiyum muṟṟiṉa vaḷḷi mutaliya kiḻaṅkukaḷiyum uṇṇumpaṭi peṟuvāy.
appāl ceṉṟāl kāntaḷ vaḷarukiṉṟa maliyil yāṉi paṭin tāṟpōla, tiruveḵkāvil pāmpilē tuyil koḷḷum tirumālik kāṇpāy. tiruveḵkāvik kaṭantu ceṉṟāl kāñci nakari aṭivāy. aṅkē cōlikaḷuḷ parikkōlik kiyilē uṭiya yāṉip pākar cōrntirukkumpōtu yāṉikaḷukku neyyuṭaṉ kalantu vikkappaṭṭa ariciyic cūluṭiya manti tiruṭik koṇṭu ceṉṟu uṇṇum. tērōṭutaliṉāl terukkaḷ tāḻntirukkiṉṟaṉa.
kaṭavuḷ uṟivatum cītēvi vāḻvatumākiya avvaracaṉiṉ viyaṉakariyaṭintu, avaṉip pala naṉmoḻikaḷāṟ pukaḻntu yāḻ vācippiṉ, avaṉ ataṉik kēṭṭu makiḻntu uṉṉuṭiya ariyiṟ kiṭanta koṭṭip pāciyiṉ vēriyotta kiḻinta cīliyip pōkkip, pālāviyi yotta, viḷaṅkukiṉṟa nūlāṟ ceyta tukilkaḷi uṉ kariya periya cuṟṟattārōṭē uṭukkap paṇṇi, vaḷinta arivāḷik koṇṭa kiyiṉiyuṭiya maṭiyaṉ ākkiṉa iṟicciyiṟ, koḻuviya tacikaḷiyum, cennel ariciyālākkiya cōṟṟiyum, iṉiya cuvi yuṭiya kaṇṭa carukkari mutaliyavaṟṟuṭaṉē ūṭṭic ciṟiyavum periyavu mākiya veḷḷik kalaṅkaḷi uṅkaḷ piḷḷikaḷukkiṭiyē parappi, tāṉ āciyuṭaṉē avarkaḷi uṇṇappaṇṇi, poṟṟāmariyi nīṇṭa mayiriṭattē aḻakupeṟac coruki, nāṉku kutirikaḷ pūṭṭiya tēriyum paciya poṉṉāṟ ceyta cēṇattiyum paricilākat taruvāṉ.

4. oḻipiyal
1. nālvaki eḻuttu
“ cīṉa pāṣiyil ovvor eḻuttum ovvor karuttik kuṟippa tāy iṉṟaḷavum pallāyiram eḻuttukkaḷi uṭiya pāṣiyāyiruk kiṟatu. tamiḻmoḻi eḻuttukkaḷiṉ āti caritamum, avvāṟē. avvā ṟeṉak kāṭṭu muṟikaḷ pala tamiḻp pūrva ilakkaṇaṅkaḷuṭ kāṇalām. yāpparuṅkala viruttiyil ‘tērpata mutaliya nālvaki yeḻuttu, ukkira veḻuttu, muttiṟaveḻuttu, katiyeḻuttu, yōṉiyeḻuttu, caṅkēta veḻuttu mutaliyaṉavāyp palavita eḻuttukkaḷiṉ peyarkaḷ kūṟappaṭ ṭuḷḷaṉa.
tivākaram piṅkalanti mutaliya nūlkaḷil:-
‘peyareḻuttu muṭiveḻuttu vaṭiveḻuttut taṉmi eḻutteṉa veḻuttiṉ peyariyam piṉarē’ (tivākaram)
“vaṭivu peyar taṇmiyuṇ muṭivu nāṉkā
naṭipeṟu nāvalar nāṭiya veḻuttē” (ilak. kot)
“uruvē yuṇarvē yoliyē taṉmi
eṉavī reḻuttu mīriru pakutiya....” (ilak. kot)
ivaṟṟuḷ ‘vaṭiveḻutteṉṟatu cittirakkuṟikaḷiyām. maṉitaṉi maṉitaṉiṉ uruvattiyēṉum avaṉiṉ pākamākiya taliyiyēṉum cittirittukkāṭṭal peyareḻutteṉṟatu caṅkētakkuṟi. pōrik kuṟikka maṉitaṉiṉ puyaṅkaḷik kāṭṭuvatu. taṉmi yeḻutteṉṟatu kuṇam, catti mutaliyavaṟṟik kāṭṭa variyum aṭiyāḷaṅkaḷ vivēkattukkuk kaṇ, veṟṟikkuk karuṭaṉ mutaliyaṉa varital. muṭiveḻutteṉṟatu viṉi nikaḻcciyi uṇarttum vākkiyaṅkaḷik kuṟikka vaḻaṅkiya vākkiyaṅkaḷ’ eṉṟār centamiḻp pattirāciriyarākiya tiru.mu.irākava aiyaṅkāravarkaḷ. tamiḻil ārāycci mikkuṭiyarāyt ‘tirāviṭa vivakārattil teḷinta cila viṣayaṅkaḷ’ eṉṉum viyācameḻutiya ep je. riccarṭs paṇṭitar, ‘ātittamiḻ eḻuttu cittira vuruvamākavē uṇṭāyirukka vēṇṭum’ eṉak kūṟi, ataṟkuc cārpākap pallāriyil mayil maliyil poṟit tirukkum cila cittiraṅkaḷiyum paṭam piṭittu accēṟṟi veḷiyiṭṭaṉar. (tamiḻ ilakkiyam pa. 18-19)
2. maṭalēṟal
“maṭalēṟalāvatu, oruvaṉ ovvāk kāmattāl paṉaṅkarukkāṟ kutiriyum, paṉantaruvi ṉuḷḷaṉavaṟṟāl vaṇṭil mutalāṉavuñ ceytu ak kutiriyiṉ mēlēṟuvatu. maṭalēṟuvāṉ tikamparaṉāy uṭaleṅkum nīṟupūcik kiḻi ōviyar kippaṭātu tāṉē tīṭṭik kiḻiyiṉ talippuṟattil avaṉ peyari varintu kippiṭittu, ūr naṭuvē nāṟcantiyil ākāra nittiriyiṉṟi, akkiḻimēṟ pārviyuñ cintiyu mirutti, vēṭki vayattaṉāy vēṟuṇarviṉṟi, āvūriṉum, aḻal mēṟpaṭiṉum aṟita liṉṟi, maḻi veyil kāṟṟāṉ mayaṅkātiruppuḻi, avvūriṉuḷḷār palaruṅ kūṭivantu nī maṭalēṟutiyō? avaḷit tarutum, cōtaṉi tarutiyō? eṉṟavaḻi, iyintāṉāyiṉ, aracaṉukkaṟivittu, avaṉē valāl avaṉ iṉintu niyattantu maṭalēṟeṉṟavaḻi, ēṟu muṟimi - pūḷi yelumpu erukku ivikaḷāṟ kaṭṭiya māliyaṇintukoṇṭu ammāvilēṟa, avvaṭatti vītiyilīrttalum, avvuruḷi yuruṇ ṭōṭumpoḻutu, paṉaṅkarukku aṟutta iṭamellām irattan tōṉṟātu vīriyantōṉṟiṉ, appoḻutu avaḷi alaṅkarittuk koṭuppatu, irattaṅ kaṇṭuḻi, avaṉik koli ceytuviṭuvatu. ivi pulavarāl nāṭṭiya vaḻakkeṉṟuṇarka.” (tañ. kōvi-uri)
“māveṉa maṭalu mūrpa pūveṉak
kuvimuki ḻerukkaṅ kaṇṇiyuñ cūṭupa
maṟuki ṉārkkavum paṭupa
piṟitu mākupa kāmaṅkāḻk koḷiṉē” (kuṟun-17)
“viḻuttalip peṇṇi viḷiyaṉ māmaṭaṉ
maṇiyaṇi peruntār mārpiṟ pūṭṭi
veḷḷeṉ paṇintupiṟa reḷḷat tōṉṟi
yorunāṇ maruṅkiṟ perunā ṇīkkit
teruvi ṉiyalavun taruvatu kollō
kaliṅkavi racinaṭip pēti
melintila ṇāmviṭaṟ kaminta tūtē” (kuṟun-182)
“ciṟumaṇi toṭarntu peruṅkaccu niṟīik
kuṟumuki ḻerukkaṅ kaṇṇi cūṭi
yuṇṇā naṉmāp paṇṇi yemmuṭaṉ
maṟukuṭaṉ tiritaruñ ciṟukuṟu mākkaḷ” (naṟṟiṉi-220)
kirāma paripālaṉam
“ceṅkaṟpaṭṭuc cillāvil, uttaramallūril, vikuṇṭa perumāḷ ālayattil ki.pi. pattāvatu nūṟṟāṇṭiṉ toṭakkattil parakēcarivarmaṉ eṉṟa cōḻaṉ ēṟpaṭuttiya oru cilācācaṉattil, uttara mallūric cērnta kirāma paripālaṉa vitikaḷic cīrtiruttiya viparam kaṇṭirukkiṟatu. atil capiyāri niyamittalāṉa oḻuṅku viḷakkiyiruk kiṟatu. periyarum ciṟiyarumāṉa kirāmavācikaḷ ōriṭattil kūṭṭaṅ kūṭa vēṇṭum. ālayaṅkaḷiṉ arccakarkaḷ yāvarum kūṭṭattil vantirukka vēṇṭum. capiyāka niyamikkattakkavarkaḷ peyarkaḷi ovvoru ōlinaṟukkil eḻuti antanta nattam vāriyākat taṉittaṉik kaṭṭukaḷākak kaṭṭi vikka vēṇṭum. arccakarkaḷil vayatu mutirntōr veṟuṅ kuṭamoṉṟik kiyileṭuttuk koṇṭu kūṭṭattiṉ mattiyil niṟka vēṇṭum. ōlik kaṭṭukaḷi ovvoṉṟākak kuṭattik kulukki oṉṟu maṟiyāta oru ciṟuvaṉi aḻittuk kuṭattil kiyiṭṭu oru naṟukki eṭukkac ceytu, ati mattiyattar kiyiṟ koṭukka vēṇṭum. anta naṟukkiluḷḷa peyari mattiyattar vācitta piṟaku maṟṟa arccakarkaḷ ovvoruvarum vācittuk kāṭṭa vēṇṭum. ataṉ mēl antantap peyar ellārukkum aṅkīrakāramākum. iṅṅaṉam nattattuk kov voruvarākak kirāma paripālaṉa makācapi ēṟpaṭuttuvateṉṟu mēṟpaṭi cācaṉattil kaṇṭirukkiṟatu.” (celvakēcavarāya mutaliyār)
1oru poṉ eṉappaṭṭa taṅka nāṇayam iraṇṭari veḷḷi nāṇayam (rūpā) peṟumatiyuḷḷatu . -tamiḻārāycci (pa.260)
3. caṅka nūlkaḷ
pattuppāṭṭu eṭṭuttoki patiṉeṇkīḻk kaṇakku mutaliyaṉa caṅka nūlkaḷeṉa vaḻaṅkappaṭukiṉṟaṉa. patiṉeṇkīḻk kaṇakkiluḷḷa nūlkaḷuṭ perumpālaṉa caṅkakālattukkup piṟpaṭṭaṉa.
“muruku porunāṟu pāṇiraṇṭu mulli
peruku vaḷamaturik kāñci-maruviṉiya
kōlaneṭu nalvāṭi kōlkuṟiñci paṭṭiṉap
pāli kaṭāttoṭum pattu”
tirumurukāṟṟuppaṭi, ciṟupāṇāṟṟuppaṭi, perumpāṇāṟ ṟuppaṭi, mullippāṭṭu, maturikkāñci, neṭunalvāṭi, kuṟiñcip pāṭṭu, paṭṭiṉappāli, malipaṭukaṭām eṉpaṉa pattuppāṭṭil aṭaṅkiya nūlkaḷām:
eṭṭuttokiyil aṭaṅkiya nūlkaḷ:
“naṟṟiṇi nalla kuṟuntoki yiṅkuṟunū
ṟotta patiṟṟuppat tōṅku paripāṭal
kaṟṟaṟintār pōṟṟuṅ kaliyō ṭakampuṟameṉ
ṟittiṟatta veṭṭut toki.”
puṟanāṉūṟu, patiṟṟuppattu, aiṅkuṟunūṟu, kalittoki, paripāṭal, akanāṉūṟu, naṟṟiṇi, kuṟuntoki eṉpaṉavām.
1. patiṉeṇ kīḻkkaṇakku nūlkaḷ
‘nālaṭi nāṉmaṇi nāṉāṟpa tintiṇimup
pālkaṭukaṅ kōvi paḻamoḻi-māmūla
meynniliya kāñciyō ṭēlāti yeṉpavē
kinniliya vāṅkīḻk kaṇakku.”
nālaṭiyār, nāṉmaṇikkaṭiki, iṉiyavi nāṟpatu, iṉṉā nāṟpatu, kār nāṟpatu, kaḷavaḻi nāṟpatu, tirikaṭukam, ācārakkōvi, paḻamoḻi, ciṟu pañcamūlam, mutumoḻikkāñci, ēlāti, aintiṇi aimpatu, tiṇi moḻi aimpatu, aintiṇi eḻupatu, tiṇimāli nūṟṟimpatu, tirukkuṟaḷ eṉpaṉavām.
kaṭiciyiṟ kūṟappaṭṭa āṟunūlkaḷip paṟṟip palvēṟu apipparāyaṅkaḷ uḷḷaṉa.
“kaṭiccaṅka nūlkaḷil peyar vaḻaṅkum pulavar kiṭṭattaṭṭa ainnūṟṟuvar eṉak kūṟappaṭṭatu. ivaruḷ perumpālār iṉṉāreṉa aṟitaṟkiṭamilli. oruvāṟu iṉṉāreṉa aṟiyak kiṭantavar irunūṟu pulavarē. ivaruḷ vēḷāḷar aimpattoruvar, peṇpālār muppat taṟuvar, antaṇar irupattoṉpatiṉmar, nākar patiṉeḻuvar; eyiṉar patiṉmūvar, kammāḷar eḻuvar, vaṇikar eḻuvar, maṉṉar aivar; āyar mūvar; kuyavar oruvar, paratavar oruvar, vaḷḷuvar oruvar, pāṇṭiya maṉṉar patiṉmūvar, cēramaṉṉar eḻuvar, cōḻamaṉṉar oruvar, toṇṭimāṉ oruvar. (tamiḻ.ilak.pa.66)
2. aimperuṅ kāppiyaṅkaḷ
cilappatikāram, cintāmaṇi, maṇimēkali, vaḷiyāpati, kuṇṭalakēci.
3. aiñciṟu kāppiyaṅkaḷ
cūḷāmaṇi, utayaṇaṉ kati, nīlakēci, yacōtara kāviyam. nākakumāra kāviyam.
4. tirukkuṟaḷ
tirukkuṟaḷ ukkirapperuvaḻuti kālattil tamiḻccaṅkattil araṅkēṟṟappaṭṭateṉpatu kaṉṉa paramparik kati. ilaṅkiyi yāṇṭa ēllāḷaṉ (ēlēlaciṅkaṉ) kālattavar ivar eṉac cilar kūṟuvar. appaṭiyāyiṉ avar kālam ki.mu.204 ākum. tiruvaḷḷuvar ceytuḷḷa nūl ellāc camayattārālum eccātiyārālum koṇṭāṭappaṭṭu varukiṉṟatu. tiruvaḷḷuvar tamatu nūliṟ kūṟiyuḷḷa camayamē tamiḻarkaḷ camayam eṉṟu palar apippapirāyappaṭukiṉṟaṉar. tiruvaḷḷuvar maṉu nūlilō cāṇakkiyar ceyta arttacāttirattilō viruntu tamatu nūlukku vēṇṭiya ātāraṅkaḷip peṟṟār eṉṟu oru cilarum, aṟṟaṉṟu, mēṟpaṭi nūlkaḷiluḷḷa palavaṟṟiyē moḻi peyart tuḷḷār eṉṟu vēṟu cilaruṅkūṟuvar. cantirakuptaṉukku mantiriyāyirunta cāṇakkiyar (kuṭaliyar) aruttacāttiram, kāmacāttiram, taruma cāttiram. mōṭca cāttiram mutaliya pala nūlkaḷi vaṭa moḻiyil cey tuḷḷār eṉṟum, avar tamiḻnāṭṭiṉar eṉṟum aṟikiṉṟōm. cāṇakkiyar tamiḻnāṭṭil aṟiyappaṭṭatum vaṭanāṭṭil aṟiyappaṭāta tumākiya poruḷkaḷip paṟṟiya nūlkaḷi iyaṟṟiṉamiyāl an nūlkaḷ vaṭanāṭṭil mikavum pukaḻ peṟṟu viḷaṅka ētuvāyiṉa. cāṇak kiyar ceytuḷḷa nūlkaḷukku ātāram tamiḻakattil nūl vaḻakkilō cevi vaḻakkilō uḷḷa poruḷkaḷi ātāramākak koṇṭu nūl iyaṟṟiya cāṇakkiyar tiruvaḷḷuvar eṉṉum iruvar nūlkaḷilum ōrōriṭaṅkaḷil orumippāṭu kāṇappaṭutal iyalpē. ākavē, vaḷḷuvar cāṇakkiyar nūliliruntu cilavaṟṟi eṭuttu moḻipeyarttuk koṇṭār eṉak kūṟutal iyipuṭiyatākātu. maṉu nūlukkum tirukkuṟaḷukkum uḷḷa vittiyācam aṇuvukkum māmēruvukkum uḷḷa tākum. itaṉ vēṟṟumiyi āciriyar cuntaram piḷḷiyavarkaḷ, “vaḷḷuvar cey tirukkuṟaḷi vaḻuvaṟa naṉ kuṇarntōhkaḷ - uḷḷuvarō maṉuvāti orukulattuk korunīti” eṉṟu naṉi viḷakkiyuḷḷār. viḷi yāṭṭuc ciṟārāl maṇaliṟ kīṟappaṭṭa kōṭukaḷilum, natti ōṭṭiluḷḷa kīṟukaḷilum ōrōviṭattu akara vaṭivam kāṇappaṭuvatu pōla maṉunūli luḷḷa ētō oru vākkiyam tirukkuṟaḷiluḷḷa yātāṉumoru poru ḷōṭu oṟṟumippaṭumāyiṉ, muṉṉatiṉ moḻipeyarppē piṉṉatu eṉṟu vātāṭutal apimāṉam paṟṟiyatākum.
5. tiruvaḷḷuvamāli
tiruvaḷḷuvariyum avar nūliyum ciṟappittup pāṭappaṭṭa veṇpākkaḷ tiruvaḷḷuvamāli eṉa vaḻaṅkum. ippāṭalkaḷ ki.pi.eṭṭām nūṟṟāṇṭu variyil yārō oru pulavarāl pāṭap paṭṭateṉac cila aṟiñar apippirāyappaṭukiṉṟaṉar.
“tiruvaḷḷuvamāli ceytatu kuṟaḷ araṅkēṟṟiya kālatteṉṟā vatu, allatu nāmakaḷ iṟiyaṉār mutaliyavarkaḷ pāṭiyaṉavākak kāṇappaṭum ceyyuḷkaḷ, meymmiyāka avarkaḷāṟ pāṭappaṭṭaṉa veṉṟāvatu koḷḷaṟkiṭamiṉmiyāl,mēṟkūṟiya māmūlaṉār pāṭ ṭeṉpatu vaḷḷuvari iḻikulattāṉeṉpataṟkut takka ātāramākātu. tiruvaḷḷuvamāli ceytatu kuṟaḷ araṅkēṟṟiya kālattaṉṟeṉpataṟkuk kāraṇam eṉṉa eṉpīrāyiṉ, acarīriyum, nāmakaḷum pāṭṭup pāṭuvatu aṉupava virōtamum acampāvitamumēyām. maṟṟiyōrkaḷāṟ pāṭap paṭṭaṉavākak kāṇappaṭum pāṭṭukkaḷuṭ perumpālaṉa meyyāka avarkaḷāṟ pāṭappaṭṭaṉavalla. eṅṅaṉameṉiṉ, oru nūlukkup pāyirañ collavō aṉṟi ataṉi eṭuttup pukaḻavō pāyirañ collavō aṉṟi ataṉip eṭuttup pukaḻavō pukkār annūṉ muḻuvatiyum paṟṟip pēcuvarēyaṉṟi, ataṉ oru pākatti māttiram pēcār. tiruvaḷḷuvamāliyilō aṟattup pālippaṟṟi,
—“pāyira nāṉkil laṟamirupāṉ paṉmūṉṟē
tūya tuṟavaṟa moṉ ṟūḻāka-āya
aṟattuppāl nālvakiyā yāynturittār nūliṉ
tiṟattuppāl vaḷḷuvaṉār tērntu.”
eṉṟu eṟiccalūr malāṭaṉārum, poruṭpālippaṟṟi,
“araciya liyin tamicciya līrin
turuval laraṇiraṇṭoṉ ṟoṇkūḻ- iruviyal
tiṇpaṭi naṭpup patiṉēḻ kuṭipatiṉmūṉ
ṟeṉporu ḷēḻā mivi”
eṉṟu pōkkiyārum, kāmattup pālippaṟṟi,
“āṇpālē ḻāṟiraṇṭu peṇpā laṭuttaṉpu
pūṇpā lirupālō rāṟāka-māṇpāya
kāmattiṉ pakkamoru mūṉṟākak kaṭṭurittār
nāmattiṉ vaḷḷuvaṉār naṉku”
eṉṟu mōcikīraṉārum kūṟiṉatāka varukiṉṟatu. immūṉṟu pāṭalum kuṟaḷiluḷḷa ovvōriyaliṉ tokiyiyum atikārattiṉ tokiyi yum kūṟukiṉṟaṉa vaṉṟik kuṟaḷiṉ ciṟappi allatu ataṉ arumi perumikaḷi eṭutturikkavilli. aṉṟic ceyyuḷaḻaku vāyntaṉa vāyumilli.ivikaḷi iyaṟṟuvataṟku mūṉṟu caṅkap pulavarkaḷ vēṇṭiyatilli; kāriki kaṟṟuk kavipāṭum oruvar pōtum. iṅṅaṉamē ivi mūṉṟum kuṟaḷiṉ iyalum atikāramum ittaṉiyeṉṟu kaṇak kiṭa virumpiya oruvarālēyē iyaṟṟappaṭṭaṉavātal vēṇṭum.
tiruvaḷḷuvamāli kuṟaḷ araṅkēṟṟappaṭṭa kālattuc ceyyap paṭavilli eṉpataṟku maṟṟoru niyāyamum kūṟutum. am māliyil ukkirapperuvaḻutiyār pāṭiṉatāka varum ceyyuḷil ‘nāṉ maṟiyiṉ meypporuḷi mupporuḷā nāṉmukattōṉ tāṉ maṟintu vaḷḷuvaṉāyt tanturitta nūl’ eṉṟum, kāvirippūmpaṭṭiṉattuk kārikkaṇṇaṉār peyar koṇṭa ceyyuḷil, ‘aiyāṟu nūṟu matikāra mūṉṟumā, meyyāya vētap poruḷ viḷaṅkap-poyyātu, tantā ṉulakukkut tāṉvaḷḷuvaṉāki, antā marimē layaṉ’ eṉṟum vaḷḷuvarip piramatēvaṉiṉ avatāramākak kūṟappaṭṭuḷḷatu. ivvāṟu vaḷḷuvarip piramatēvaṉiṉ avatāramākak kūṟavantatu avar uyirō ṭiruntapōtaṉṟu. uyirōṭirukkumpōtē oruvarik kaṭavuḷiṉ avatāramākak koṇṭāṭuvatu eṅkumilli. oruvar iṟantapiṉ pala varuṭaṅkaḷ ceṉṟu kālam nīṭikka nīṭikkattāṉ avaruṭiya perumi vaḷarcciyuṟṟut teyvat taṉmiyaṭiyum. appōtutāṉ avar kaṭavuḷiṉ avatāramākak karutappaṭuvār. aṅṅaṉamē vaḷḷuvariṉ aṟiviṉ perumiyik kaṇṭu avari oru kaṭavuḷiṉ avatāramākak koṇṭāṭa vantatu avar kuṟaḷiyaṟṟip pala varuṭaṅkaḷ ceṉṟapiṉpē yām. appaṭic ciṟitu kālañ ceṉṟu vaḷḷuvar piramatēvaṉuṭiya avatārameṉṟa koḷki vērūṉṟiyataṉ piṉṉarē intap pāṭṭukkaḷ pāṭappaṭṭiruttal vēṇṭum. iṅṅaṉam ippāṭalkaḷ pāṭappaṭṭatu kuṟaḷ iyaṟṟiya veku kālattukkup piṉpāṉāl, avaṟṟi aṭakkiyuḷḷa tiruvaḷḷuvamāli kuṟaḷaraṅkēṟṟappaṭṭa kālattu iyaṟṟappaṭṭa tākātu.
iṉi iṟiyaṉār kaḷaviyaluriyil kaṭic caṅkap pulavar eṇmar peyarkaḷ kūṟappaṭṭirukkiṉṟaṉa. ivveṇmaruḷ tiruvaḷḷuvamāli yil mūvar peyar māttiram kāṇappaṭukiṉṟaṉa; aivar peyarilli. aṉṟiyum cilappatikārattila makā makōpāttiyāyar cāmināta aiyaravar kaḷāl eṭuttuk kāṭṭappaṭṭa paḻiya akavaloṉṟu kaṭiccaṅkap pulavaril irupatteḻuvar peyar kūṟukiṉṟatu. avaṟṟil patiṉēḻu peyar tiruvaḷḷuvamāliyil illi. itaṉāl tiruvaḷḷuvamāli pāṭiṉavar kaṭiccaṅkap pulavarkaḷ eṉṟu colvataṟkiṭamiṉṟākiṉṟatu.
mēṟkūṟiyavaṟṟāl tiruvaḷḷuvamāli kaṭiccaṅkakālattu nūlaṉṟeṉpatum, atiluḷḷa pāṭṭoṉṟēṉum aṅkē kūṟappaṭṭa pulavarālēyē iyaṟṟappaṭṭateṉpataṟkut takka ātāram illi eṉpatum naṉku pōtarum. āyiṉ tiruvaḷḷuvamāli ekkālattu yārāl iyaṟṟappaṭṭateṉiṉ, kālantōṟum tirukkuṟaḷip paṟṟip pāṭṭukkaḷ pala pulavarāliyaṟṟappaṭṭaṉavāka, piṟkālattu vanta pulavaroruvar avaṟṟiyellām, aṉṟēl, avaṟṟuṭ ciṟantavaṟṟi māttiram oruṅku cērttu oḻuṅkupaṭutti oru nūlākkiyiruttal vēṇṭum. avar tāmum cila pāṭṭukkaḷ pāṭic cērttirukkalām. allatu nūṉ muḻuvatiyum oru pulavarē ceytumirukkalām. vaḷḷuvamāliyil varum pāṭṭukkaḷ ēṟakkuṟiya orē vitamāṉa nērici veṇpākkaḷāka viruntālum, acarīri coṉṉatāka varum pāṭṭu acarīriyē nērē coṉṉa tāyillāmal,
“tiruttaku teyvat tiruvaḷ ḷuvarō
ṭuruttaku naṟpalaki yokka-irukka
uruttira caṉma reṉavurittu vāṉil
orukkavō veṉṟatōr col”
eṉa, acarīri coṉṉatāka maṟṟoruvarāl pāṭappaṭṭiruttalum intak koḷkikkut takunta tuṇiyākum.
iṉṉum intap pāṭṭu maturiyil teyvattaṉmi poruntiya caṅkappalaki oṉṟiruntateṉpatik kuṟittaliṉālum, antap palakiyil tiruvaḷḷuvarō ṭokkaviruttaṟku uruttiracaṉmarē ēṟṟavareṉpata ṉāl, kuṟaḷaraṅkēṟṟiya kālattil caṅkappulavar nāṟpatteṇmarum tiruvaḷḷuvarō ṭokkavirukkat takutiyaṟṟavarāyp poṟṟāmari vāviyuḷ vīḻntu mūḻkiṉareṉṉuṅ katikkut tōṟṟumiṭamā yiruttali ṉālum, itu kuṟaḷ tōṉṟi veku kālattukkup piṉ iyaṟṟappaṭṭirukka vēṇṭumeṉpatu tēṟṟam. caṅkap palakiyilēṟṟiya intak kati kaṭṭukkati. itu pōṉṟatoru kati caṭakōpariyaṟṟiya tiruvāy moḻiyippaṟṟiyum vaḻaṅkukiṉṟatu.” (tirukkōṇamali vittuva cirōmaṇi ta.kaṉakacuntaram piḷḷi. pi.ē.)
1tiruvaḷḷuvamāli eṉṉum nūl cantēkattukkuriya katikaḷum kaṟpaṉikaḷum niṟinta pāṭalkaḷ aṭaṅkiyatu. ippāṭalkaḷ ki.pi. 9ām nūṟṟāṇṭaḷavil tiruvaḷḷuvar nūlic ciṟappikka yārō oru tamiḻppulavarāl pāṭappaṭṭiruttal vēṇṭum. - tamiḻārāycci pa.247
4. makaḷiric ciṟippiṭittal
muṉṉāḷil ōr aracaṉ piṟaṉōr aracaṉōṭu porutuveṉ ṟāl, avaṉ nāṭṭiluḷḷa makaḷiric ciṟippiṭittu vanta avarik kaṟpaḻittum māṉapaṅkañ ceytum varuttāmal pātukāttuk kōyil kaḷil kaṭavuḷit toḻutukoṇṭu aṅkuk kaṭavuṭṭiruppaṇi ceyyumpaṭi iruttuvāṉ.
5. kaṭaṟpōr
cēralātaṉ kaṭaliṭattē tīvoṉṟil vacitta taṉ pakivarmēṟ kappaṟpaṭiyuṭaṉ ceṉṟu, avaratu kāvaṉ maramāṉa kaṭampi veṭṭi yeṟintu, avaṉip pōr tolittāṉ. ivvaralāṟu patiṟṟuppattil kūṟappaṭukiṉṟatu.
6. tamiḻaracar mālikaḷ
pāṇṭiyaṉ-vēmpu, cēraṉ-paṉi, cōḻaṉ-ātti. “pōnti vēmpē āreṉa varūum māperuntāṉiyar”(tol)
7. paṭikaḷiṉ aṇivakuppu mutaliyaṉa
aṇi, uṇṭi, ōṭṭu, ākkam, koṭippaṭi, tār, tūci, niṟi, kūḻi.
8. akaḻikaḷ
cila akaḻikaḷ 40 aṭit tāḻvu iruntaṉa. ki.pi. 781 ilum 977 ilum eḻutappaṭṭa cila cilācācaṉaṅkaḷ itik kuṟippiṭukiṉṟaṉa. - (Studies in Tamil Literature and History p.23)
9. pōrmuracu
ceṅkuṭṭuvaṉ kaṉakavicayaruṭaṉ yuttattukkup puṟappaṭṭapōtu koṭumpaṟi, neṭuvayir, muracam, pāṇṭil, mayirkkaṇ muracam mutaliyaṉa olikkappaṭṭaṉa eṉṟu cilappatikārattiṟ kūṟappaṭṭuḷḷatu.
10. ilavantikippaḷḷi
aracaṉ araṇmaṉiyil vācañceyvatu māttiramallāmal cōlikaḷiṉ mattiyil amikkappaṭṭuḷḷa kuḷircci poruntiya māḷikiyilum kōṭikkālaṅkaḷil vacittāṉ. ammāḷiki ilavantikippaḷḷi eṉṟu vaḻaṅkappaṭṭatu. ataṉkaṇ kāṟṟu vacatiyum nīr vacatiyum porunta amikkappaṭṭuḷḷa maṇṭapam ‘nīrāḻi maṇṭapam’ eṉappaṭṭatu.
11. aracaṉit terivu ceyyum nūtaṉa muṟi
ōr aracaṉiṉ maraṇattukkuppiṉ irācciyapāram ēṟpavar illātapoḻutum, irācciyattiṉ urimikkup palar vātāṭum poḻutum paṭṭattuyāṉi vāyilāka aracaṉit terivu ceytalum muṉṉāḷ vaḻakku eṉpatu,
“kaḻumalat tiyātta kaḷiṟum karuvūr
viḻumiyōṉ mēṟceṉ ṟataṉāl-viḻumiya
vēṇṭiṉum vēṇṭā viṭiṉum uṟaṟpāla
tīṇṭā viṭutal aritu” (paḻamoḻi-26)
eṉṉum paḻamoḻi veṇpāvāl aṟiyak kiṭakkiṉṟatu.
12. araca taṇṭaṉikaḷ
kaḷavu, viyapicāram, irācatturōkam mutaliyavaṟṟukkut takunta taṇṭaṉi ēṟpaṭuttappaṭṭiruntatu. uṟuppukkaḷi veṭṭutal, ciṟiyil aṭittal, koli ceytal eṉat taṇṭam mūṉṟu vakippaṭṭatu. irācat turōkattukkuk koliyum, viyapicārattiṟkuk kāl kuṟitta lum muṟiyākaviruntaṉa.
“kāṇiṟ kuṭippaḻiyāṅ kiyuṟiṟ kāl kuṟiyum” (nālaṭi. 34)
“koliyiṟ koṭiyāri vēntoṟuttal piṅkūḻ
kaḷikaṭ ṭataṉoṭu nēr” (kuṟaḷ. 550)
13. kirāmac caṅkam
kirāma kāriyaṅkaḷi vayatiṉ mutiyavarkaḷ ūr naṭuviluḷḷa maraṅkaḷiṉ kīḻk kūṭi muṭivu ceytārkaḷ. aṅṅaṉam kūṭum caṅkam maṉṟam allatu potiyil eṉṟu aṟiyappaṭṭatu. immaṉṟaṅkaḷ perum pālum, āl, viḷā, palā, vēmpu mutaliya maraṅkaḷiṉkīḻk kūṭiṉa. marattic cūḻntu nāṟpuṟamum tiṇṇi amikkappaṭṭiruntatu.
14. tuṟavikaḷ tōṟṟam
maravuriyi uṭiyākac ceyta uṭiyiṉar. aḻakālum vaṭivālum niṟattālum valampuric caṅkiyokkum nari muṭiyiṉar. ekkālattum nīrāṭutaliṉ aḻukkillāmal viḷaṅkum mēṉiyar. aṭikkaṭi viratamiruntu paṭṭiṉi kiṭappatāl taciyillāta mārpiṉ elumpukaḷiṉ kōvi veḷippaṭat tōṉṟi ulavum uṭampiṉiyuṭiyavar. epporuḷum nukarvataṟku naṉṟākiya pakaṟpoḻutukaḷ pala cērakkaḻinta vuṇaviṉi yuṭiyār. neṭuṅkālam maṉattil taṅkakkūṭiya kōpattip pōkkiya maṉattiṉar. palavaṟṟiyum kaṟṟa perum kalvimāṉkaḷum ciṟitum aṟiya māṭṭāta iyalpāṉa aṟivuṭiyavar. palavaṟṟiyum kaṟṟōrkkut tām elliyākiya talimiyiyuṭiyavar. āciyōṭē kūṭiya ciṉatti yum pōkkiṉa aṟiviṉiyuṭiyavar. tavattāl uṭalukku varuttam iruppatu uṇmiyeṉiṉum taṅkaḷ maṉattiṉāl varuttam ciṟitu maṟiyappaṭāta iyalpiṉiyuṭiyavar. (tirumurukāṟṟuppaṭi)
15. makaḷir aḻaku
oru pāṭiṉiyiṉ aḻakiṉ viparam porunarāṟṟuppaṭiyil kūṟappaṭukiṉṟatu. atu varumāṟu: kūntal vārnta āṟṟiṉ karumaṇal pōṉṟatu. neṟṟi piṟi pōṉṟatu. puruvam viṟpōṉṟatu, kuḷircci poruntiya kaṇ aḻakiya kaṭiyiyuṭiyatu, vāy ilavitaḻ pōṉṟatu, paṟkaḷ veḷḷiya muttukkaḷ pōṉṟaṉa, kātukaḷ kattiriki pōṉṟaṉa. avi makarakkuḻi aṇiyappeṟṟu aciyun taṉmiyuṭiyaṉa, tōḷkaḷ mūṅkilpōṟ perumiyuṭiyaṉa, muṉkikaḷ aitākiya mayiriṉiyuṭiyaṉa, viralkaḷ kāntaḷmalar pōṉṟaṉa, nakaṅkaḷ kiḷiyiṉatu vāy pōṉṟaṉa, mulikaḷ īrkkiṭi pōkātu paṇittaṉa, koppūḻ nīrccuḻi pōṉṟatu, iṭi ciṟumiyiyuṭiyatu. alkul palamaṇi kōtta mēkali aṇiyappaṭṭatu, kuṟaṅku piṭiyiṉatu ki pōṉṟatu. aṭikaḷ ōṭi iḷitta nāyiṉatu nākkup pōṉṟaṉa, koṇṭi vāḻippūp pōṉṟatu.
16. tamiḻar vāṇipam
1paḻiya tamiḻ ilakkiyaṅkaḷum kirēkka urōmaṉ nūlāciriyar kaḷum kiṟittu muṉṉiraṇṭu nūṟṟāṇṭukaḷilum cōḻa irācciyattic cērnta kiḻakkukkari kiḻakkut tēcaṅkaḷōṭum mēṟkut tēcaṅkaḷōṭum periya vāṇipam naṭattuvataṟkiṭaṉāyiruntatu. cōḻa tēcak kaṭa lōṭikaḷ kariyōraṅkaḷil māttiram pirayāṇañceytārallar. avarkaḷ vaṅkāḷak kuṭākkaṭalik kaṭantu kaṅkānati airāvati mukattu vāraṅkaḷukkuc ceṉṟārkaḷ; intu camuttirattik kaṭantu malāyt tīvuk kūṭṭaṅkaḷi aṭintārkaḷ. (viṉceṉṭ cimit)
17. nakar kāvalar
ivar pulipōlunar. tuyirkoḷḷāta kaṇṇar. añcāneñcar. kaḷavutoḻil tantiraṅkaḷik kaṇṭaṟiyum nuṇṇiya aṟiviṉar. kuṟitappā ampiṉar. terukkaḷil maḻinīr perukkeṭuttōṭum kāriruḷ kavinta yāmaṅkaḷilum ivar iḷippiṉṟi ūr cuṟṟi varuvār. kaṟutta uṭaliṉa rāyk karuñcēli yuṭuttu vāḷ kikkoṇṭu meṉṉūlēṇi ariyiṟ cuṟṟi viḻitta kaṇ imikku muṉ maṟintōṭum valiyuṭiya kaḷvariyum kaṭitiṟ piṭittukkoṇarum āṟṟaluṭiyavar. (maturik kāñci)
18. iṉṉa pulavar iṉṉa pāvil tiṟamiyuṭiyāreṉpatu.
“veṇpāviṟ pukaḻēnti paraṇikkōr
cayaṅkoṇṭāṉ virutta meṉṉum
oṇpāvi luyarkampaṉ kōviyulā
antātik koṭṭak kūttaṉ
kaṇpāya kalampakattiṟ kiraṭṭiyarkaḷ
vacipāṭak kāḷa mēkam
paṇpāya pakarcantam paṭikkāca
lātoruvar pakaro ṇātē.”
19. tamiḻ ārāycci1 nūliṟ kāṇum cila kuṟippukkaḷ
2āriyar varukikku muṉṉamēyē eḻuttuc cuvaṭi mutaliya tamiḻc coṟkaḷ uṇmiyāl akattiyar vaṭakkiliruntu eḻuttik koṇṭu vantār eṉpatu uṇmiyaṉṟu. -tamiḻārāycci pakkam 122.
payirttoḻil, aṅkanūl, ciṟpakkali, kaṭṭaṭak kali, vāṉanūl, vāṇikam, vīṭṭupporuḷkaḷ, uṟaviṉ muṟikaḷ, vilaṅkukaḷ. paṟavikaḷ. tāvaraṅkaḷ, moḻi, ilakkiyam maruntu, ulōkaṅkaḷ, araciyal, camayam, yuttam, niṟi , aḷavu mutaliyavikaḷum piṟavumākiya vaṟṟip pulappaṭuttum paḻiya tamiḻc coṟkaḷuṇṭu.
20. ēṟu taḻuvutal
ēṟu taḻuvutal āyarukkē uriyatāyiruntatu. piṟi pōṉṟa kōṭṭiṉiyuṭiya ēṟṟiṉ valiyi aṭakkiya ciṟuvarukku āyar tam makaḷiri vatuvi cūṭṭiṉar. ēṟu taḻuvuṅkāl avvēṟukaḷ taṅ kūrṅkōṭukaḷāl ciṟuvarkaḷiṉ vayiṟṟip piḷantu avarik kolvatu muṇṭu. itaṉ viparam mullik kaliyil naṉku kūṟappaṭṭuḷḷatu.
“avviṭattu ēṟukaḷit taḻuvippōkkumpaṭiyi uṭkoṇṭu vantu tiraṇṭu maḻi muḻakkeṉṉa iṭiyeṉṉa naṭuvāṉilattiṉ muṉṉē āravārameḻap pukiyōṭu pukiyeḻa ēṟu taḻuviṉārkkuk koṭuttaṟku nalla makaḷir tiraṇṭu niṟpa, nīrt tuṟiyilum ālamarattiṉ kīḻum valiyiṉiyuṭiya marāmarattiṉ kīḻum uṟiyun teyvaṅkaṭkuc ceyyum muṟimikaḷip paravit toḻuvilē pāyntār.
“erutiṉatu nōkki añcāṉāy ataṉmēl pāynta iṭi yaṉic cāvak kuttik kompiṭattē eṭuttukkoṇṭu uṭalik kulik kiṉṟa tōṟṟaravik kāṇāy; aḻakiya talimayiriṉiyuṭiya maṉamacinta iyalpiṉi yuṭiyaḷākiya turōpatiyatu tuyya kūntalilē kiyi nīṭṭiya tuccātaṉaṉuṭiya neñcippiḷantu pōkaṭṭup pakivar naṭuvē tāṉ coṉṉa vañciṉatti vāykkac ceyta vīmacēṉaṉip pōlum” (kali. 101)
“ēṟākiya erumiyi yēṟukiṉṟa kūṟṟuvaṉuṭiya neñci vaṭimpālē piḷantu pōkaṭṭuc ciṉattōṭē ariya uyiri vāṅkiṉa aññāṉṟi yiṟivaṉ ittaṉmiyi uṭiyavaṉko leṉṟu kūṟumpaṭiyākak kāṟṟiṉ vicipōla ōṭi vantu virintu ēṟākiya kāriyataṉip palarum vantu cērtaliyuṭiya kaḷattē valiyaṭaṅkat taḻuvi varutti ataṉmēlē tōṉṟi niṉṟa potuvaṉatu aḻakip pārāy; ataṉik kaṇṭu eṉ neñcu uṭkiṟṟukkā ṇeṉṟāḷ.” (kali. 103)
21. paḻiya maṇamuṟi
cōṟu neyyuṭaṉ mutiyōrukku aḷikkappaṭṭatu. paṭcikaḷ naṉṉimittaṅ kāṭṭiṉa. periya vāṉam kaḷaṅkamiṉṟi yiruntatu. cantiraṉ kuṟṟamillātu rōkiṇiyuṭaṉ poruntiyiruntāṉ. maṇappantal alaṅkarikkappaṭṭatu. teyvatti vaḻipaṭṭārkaḷ. periya muḻavu olittatu. nīrāṭṭi alaṅkarikkappaṭṭa maṇappoṇṇi ēṉiya peṇkaḷ imikoṭṭātu nōkkiṉārkaḷ. kaḻuvappaṭṭa pūvitaḻ kaḷāṟ ceyyappaṭṭa vaṇaṅkutaṟkuriya teyvavaṭivam vākippūviṉ mēlum aṟukampulliṉ mēlum vikkappaṭṭatu. atu pū moṭṭu kaḷālum veṇṇūlālum alaṅkarikkappaṭṭup puṉitamākiya uṭi tarikkappaṭṭatu. maḻittuḷikaḷ pōṉṟa maṇal cintappaṭṭa pantaliṉ kīḻt talivi vīṟṟiruntāḷ. āparaṇap poṟiyiṉāl cōrvaṭinta maṇappeṇṇiṉ viyarvi kāyumpaṭi cuṟṟattāh viciṟiyiṉāl vīci avaḷit talivaṉukku aḷittārkaḷ. (akam. 136)
palar uṇṭapiṉṉum uḻuntuṭaṉ camikkappaṭṭa cōṟṟuk kuviyal iruntatu. niritta kālkaḷiṉmēṟkaṭṭappaṭṭa pantaliṉkīḻp putiya maṇalip parappiṉārkaḷ. vīṭṭil viḷakkukaḷ ēṟṟappaṭṭaṉa. talivaṉum taliviyum pūmālikaḷāl alaṅkarikkappaṭṭārkaḷ. naṭcattiraṅkaḷ tīya palaṉik koṭāta nalla vaḷarpiṟip pakkattē viṭiyaṟkāliyil cila peṇkaḷ kuṭaṅkaḷit tāṅkavum, maṟṟavarkaḷ putiya akaṉṟa kuṭaṅkaḷi ovvoruvarāka māṟavum, vayatiṉ mutirnta peṇkaḷ perum āravārañ ceytārkaḷ. putalvarip payanta aḻakiya āparaṇaṅkaḷiyaṇinta peṇkaḷ pūvitaḻum nellum iṭappaṭṭa nīri maṇappeṇṇiṉ kariya kūntal pirakācikkumpaṭi peytu, ‘kaṟpu neṟiyil niṉṟu kaṇavaṉukkut tuṇiyāy vāḻvāyāka’ eṉṟu vāḻttiṉar. maṇaccaṭaṅku niṟivēṟiya irākkālattilē ayalilē uḷḷa peṇkaḷ kūṭi maṇamakaḷip putiya āṭi mutaliyavaṟṟāl alaṅkarittut taliva ṉiṭattuykka avaḷ nāṇattiṉāloṭuṅkiṉāḷ. (akam. 86)
22. pēymakaḷ
kāynta mayiriṉiyum, niri ovvāta palliṉiyum, periya vāyiṉiyum, kōpattāṟ cuḻalukiṉṟa kaṇṇiṉiyum koṭiya pārviyiṉiyum, pituṅkiya kaṇṇiyumuṭiya kūkiyōṭē kaṭiya pāmpu tūṅkukiyiṉālē periya muliyi varuttukiṉṟa kātiṉiyum, kuṟipāṭuṭiya uṭaliṉiyum kaṇṭār añcum naṭiyiṉiyumuṭiya veruvarun tōṟṟattiyuṭiya pēymakaḷ utiratti yaḷinta kūriya ukiriṉiyuṭiya koṭiya viralālē kaṇkaḷit tōṇṭi, uṇṇap paṭṭa mikka muṭināṟṟattiyuṭiya kariya taliyi oḷi poruntiya vaḷiyalaṇinta kiyilē eṭuttu veṟṟik kaḷattip pāṭit. tōḷi yacittu, niṇattit tiṉkiṉṟa vāykaḷāyt tuṇaṅkik kūttāṭuvāḷ.
23. cila nampikkikaḷ
kākaṅ karivatu viruntiṉar varuvataṟkaṟikuṟiyeṉak karutappaṭṭatu. puṭkarital allatu avi paṟakkun ticikaḷ tām uṉṉic cellum karumaṅkaḷukku anukūlam allatu piratikūlam ēṟpaṭuvati uṇarttum eṉa nampap paṭṭatu. takka paricil peṟāviṭattup pulavarkaḷ puḷḷiyum poḻutiyum paḻippārkaḷ (puṟam-204), pēykaḷ maraṅkaḷilum iṭukāṭu cuṭukāṭukaḷilum uṟiyum. pēymakaḷir pōrkkaḷaṅkaḷiṟ ceṉṟu iṟantavarkaḷiṉ kāyaṅkaḷil viralkaḷi aḷintu avaṟṟāl taṅkaḷ taliyiṉik kōtic cenniṟamāy viḷaṅkiṉārkaḷ. avarkaḷ piṇaṅ kaḷit taḻuvip pulāliṉip pucittārkaḷ. pēykaḷi ōṭṭuvataṟku veṇ kaṭukip pukippittal muṉṉuḷḷōr vaḻakku. pēy makaḷir maṉita niṇat tilum irattattilum viruppuḷḷōrāvar. ākavē, avi puṇpaṭṭa pōrvīraric cūḻntu tiriyum. avi puṇṇit tīṇṭiṉāl puṇ āṟātu. puṇpaṭṭōṉ iṟantu viṭuvāṉ. taṉ kaṇavaṉiṉ puṇṇip pēy tīṇṭātapaṭi avaṉuṭiya maṉivi vēppili iravam ilikaḷi vīṭṭiṉ iṟappil ceruki, yāḻ mutaliya icikkaruvikaḷi olippittu veṇkaṭuku citaṟi, kāñcipāṭi, vācaṉip poruḷkaḷip pukip pippāḷ.
“tīṅkaṉi yiravamoṭu vēmpumaṉic cerīi
vāṅkumarup piyāḻoṭu palliyaṅ kaṟaṅkak
kipayap peyarttu miyiḻu tiḻuki
yiyavi citaṟi yāmpa lūti
yicimaṇi yeṟintu kāñci pāṭi
neṭunakar varippiṟ kaṭinaṟi pukii” (puṟam-281)
24. talikkōl
poṉ uṟiyiṭṭu, muttukkaḷum navamaṇikaḷum aḻutti alaṅkarikkappaṭṭa mūṅkiṟkōl oṉṟu araṅkiṉ mattiyil vikkap paṭṭiruntatu. im mūṅkiṟkōl perumpālum paki aracaṉiṉ veṇ koṟṟak kuṭiyiṉ kāmpāka virukkum. aḵtu araṇmaṉiyiṉ ōr aṟiyil vaṇakkattukkuriyatāka vikkappaṭum. kūttu nikaḻum nāḷil atu poṟkuṭaṅkaḷil niṟikkappaṭṭa nīrāl muḻukkāṭṭiyapiṉ pūmāliyālum muttumālikaḷālum alaṅkarikkappaṭṭup, paṭṭattu yāṉiyil ēṟṟi, ūrvalañ ceyyappaṭṭu. araṅkiṉ mattiyil nāṭṭap paṭum. āṭṭan toṭaṅkumuṉ kūttiyar talikkōli vaṇaṅkutal marapu. talikkōl kūttiyarkku aḷikkappaṭum paṭṭamumām.
25. parattiyar
paṭṭiṉaṅkaḷil parattiyar cērikaḷ iruntaṉa. kalittokiyil ēṉātippāṭi eṉa ōriṭattiṟ kūṟappaṭukiṉṟatu. iḵtu ēṉātippaṭṭam peṟṟā ṉoruvaṉ ēṟpaṭuttiya parattiyar cēriyeṉa ataṉuriyāl viḷaṅkukiṉṟatu. parattiyar āṭalpāṭalkaḷiṟ ciṟantu kaṇṇukku miyeḻutik kaṇṭār mayaṅkumpaṭi tammi alaṅkarittu viḷaṅku vārkaḷ. avarkaḷ āṭavarit tammuṭaṉ ērikaḷilum poykikaḷilum āṭumpaṭitūṇṭuvārkaḷ. kuṟuntokiyil oru parattiyiṉ kūṟṟākak kūṟappaṭuvatu varumāṟu:
nāṅkaḷ āmpaliṉ taḻiyic cūṭi nīrāṭac celvōm. avaḷ (maṉivi) eṅkaḷiṭattil accaṅkoḷvāḷāyiṉ tāṉum taṉatu cuṟṟat tārum eḻiṉiyiṉ paṭiyip pōl, avaṉik kāttukkoḷḷaṭṭum, (kuṟun-80) viḻākkālaṅkaḷil avarkaḷ āṭavari mayakkuvataṟkuk kūṭiṉārkaḷ. “āmpal iliyiṉāl alkuli aḻaku peṟa alaṅkarittu viḻāvukkuc celvēṉ. intac ceḻumi mikka nāṭṭiṉ talivaṉ kāṇpāṉāyiṉ, eṉṉi varikkāmal viṭutal aritākum.” (naṟ-390) maṉiviyar taṅ kaṇavarip parattiyar mayakkik koṇṭu cellātapaṭi kāttārkaḷ.
“maṭakkaṇ takarak kūntaṟ paṇittōḷ
vārnta vāleyiṟṟuc cērntuceṟi kuṟaṅkiṟ
piṇiya lantaḻit tiit tuṇiyilaḷ
viḻavukkaḷam poliya vantuniṉ ṟaṉaḷē
eḻumiṉō veḻumiṉeṅ koḻunaṟ kākkam” (naṟ-170)
“ap pōtaravu pārttirunta parattiyar kuḻalūti yāḻeḻīit taṇṇumi yiyakki muḻaviyampit talimakaṉi iṅkuk kūttuṇ ṭeṉpatu aṟivippa.” eṉṉi?
‘kuḻaleḻīi yāḻeḻīit taṇṇumip piṉṉar
muḻaviyampa lāman tiriki’
eṉṟu kūttunū luṭiyāruñ coṉṉā reṉpatu. avvaki aṟivik kappaṭṭa talimakaṉ nām itaṉi oru kāl nōkkip pōtumeṉṟu cellum. ceṉṟakkāl avarkaḷ taṅkaṇ tāḻvippa reṉpatu. (iṟi-akapporuḷuri)
26. caṅka nūlkaḷāl aṟiyum tamiḻar nākarikam
1. pattuppāṭṭāl aṟiyappaṭuvaṉa:
aṟukampullāl tiritta paḻutiyittiṉṟa cemmaṟikkiṭāyiṉatu iṟicci, irumpuk kampiyil kōttuc cuṭṭa iṟicci, pakalu miravum iṟicciyit tiṉṟu paṟkaḷ muṉi maḻuṅkutal, nālu kutiri pūṭṭiya tēr, ēḻaṭipiṉ cellal, tēṉākiya neyyōṭē kiḻaṅkiyum viṟṟavarkaḷ, mīṉiṉatu neyyōṭē naṟaviyum koṇṭupōtal, vāḻippūveṉap polinta vōti, umaṇarōṭu vanta manti ciṟu piḷḷikaḷōṭu kilukilu viḷiyāṭutal, amiḻtuviḷi tīṅkaṉi oḷavikkīnta atikaṉ, aravu vekuṇṭaṉṉa naṟavu nalkal, āypāmpu koṭutta nīlappaṭṭāṭiyi ālamar celvarkkuk koṭuttatu, kaṟṟōṟttuṭutta paṭivappārppāṉ, pāvi viḷakku, periya koṭi pāymarattilē yāṭukiṉṟa marakkalam, puli kūṭṭilē aṭikkappaṭṭiruttal, māṟupāṭṭiyēṟṟuk kōṟaṟ ṟoḻiliyuṭiya ēṟṟiṉatu cevvittōli mayir cīvāmaṟ pōrtta muracam olittal, ilaṅku iḻi makaḷir polamkalattu ēntiya maṇaṅ kamaḻ tēṟal maṭuppa makiḻntuṇṭal, irumpuceyviḷakku, kañcitōytta tukil, naṭuniciyil vēppiliyit taliyilē kaṭṭiṉa vēliyuṭiya vēntaṉ pācaṟiyiṟ puṇpaṭṭōrip parikarittal, kāri uṇṭik kaṭavuḷ.
2. akanāṉūṟṟālaṟiyappaṭuvaṉa:
naṭukal vaḻipāṭu, puli, tāṉ koṉṟa yāṉi iṭappakkam vīḻntāl māṉattiṉāl ataṉi uṇṇāmi, makaḷir kuṟiñcip pāṭṭik kēṭṭut tiṉi mēya vanta yāṉi uṟaṅkuvatu, vēṅkimalar koyyum makaḷir puli puli eṉṟa oliyik kēṭṭup puliyi ōṭṭuvataṟku āṭavar villuṅ kompum eṭuttukkoṇṭu vantu puliyik kāṇāmal veḷkip pōtal, talivaṉ parattiyoṭu kāviriyil nīr viḷiyāṭutal, kaṇavar varavik karutic cuvariṟ kōṭiṭṭu nāḷ eṇṇutal, peṇ āmiyīṉṟa muṭṭiyi āṇ āmi pātukāttal.
3. kuṟantokiyālaṟiyappaṭuvaṉa:
āviṉ kaḻuttil maṇi yaṇital, makaḷir vaṇṭalayartal, kuṟacciṟār kuṟi collutal, ciṟu piḷḷikaḷ ciṟutēruruṭṭi viḷiyāṭutal, pulippaṟṟāli ciṟārk kaṇital, peṇkaḷ tuṇaṅkikkūttāṭutal, vaṇṇātti kañcippaci pōṭutal, viḷiyāṭṭu makaḷir maṇal vīṭukaṭṭutal, nuḷicciyar nel peṟṟu upput tarutal, paratavar kaṭṭu marattiliruntu mīṉ piṭittal, alavaṉ taṉatu peṭiyi nīrāṭṭutal, aṉṟil paṉimaṭaliṟ kūṭukaṭṭutal, kuyil mūṅkiliṟ ṟaṅkutal, cūlmakaḷir puḷippi virumputal, vāḷi mīṉukkuk kompiruttal, kiḷi vēppam paḻattik kavvutal, yāṉi taṉakkuc ceyta tīṅki maṟavāmi, yāṉi vāḻittaṇṭāl matamaḻital, tuyarāl varuṅ kaṇṇīrveppamātal.
4. naṟṟiṇiyālaṟiyappaṭuvaṉa:
kātali cuvarilē kōṭiṭṭup pirinta talimakaṉ varunāḷ eṇṇu tal, kālāṟ pantu viḷiyāṭutal, kātalaṉ varavip palli kūṟutal, irumpu kāycciya uliyilē kollaṉ paṉimaṭaliluḷḷa nīr teḷittu neruppi aṇittal, erumi mēyppāṉ ataṉmēl ēṟiccellal.
5. kalittokiyiṟ kāṇappaṭum ceytikaḷ:
mukkaṇṇāṉ mūveyili erittatu (pāli-2), ‘eṟittaru katir tāṅkiyēntiya kuṭinīḻa, luṟittāḻnta karakamu muricāṉṟa mukkōlu, neṟippaṭac cuvalaciivēṟōrā neñcattuk, kuṟippēval ceyaṉ mālik koḷinaṭiyantaṇar’ (pāli-9) palkalic cilpūṅ kaliṅkattaḷ (kuṟi-56) tiyil nīrāṭiya tavam (kuṟi-59) ōttuṭi yantaṇaṉ erivalañ ceyvāṉ pōl (maru-66) ‘kiḷarmaṇiyārppārppac cāayc cāyayc cellun taḷarnaṭic ciṟār’(maru-80), ālamar celvaṉ aṇicāl peruviṟal (81) ālamar celvaṉaṇicāl makaṉ viḻā(83) cuṟāvē ṟeḻutiṉa mōtiram (maru-84), tērivāyk kiṇkiṇi (maru-86) mukam kutiri mukamum uṭal makkaḷuṭalumākiya iraṇṭu aḻakukkup poruntiya cūrapaṉmāvikkoṉṟa civanta vēliyuṭiya murukaṉ(93), kuṟumpūḻp pōr (95), toyyil poṟitta vaṉamuli (maru-97), mīṉpūt tavirvaru mantivāṉ vicumpupōl-vāṉpoṟi paranta puḷḷi veḷḷi yuṅ-kolivaṉ cūṭiya kuḻavittiṅkaḷ pōl-vaḷipumalinta kōṭaṇi cēy (103), perum peyark kaṇicciyōṉ maṇimiṭaṟṟaṇi pōl (105), neruppukki toṭṭavar pōla vitirttiṭṭu (115), tampukaḻ kēṭṭār pōṟ ṟalicāyttu (119), mukkōlko ḷantaṇar mutumoḻi niṉivār pōl (126), uṇkaṭaṉ vaḻimoḻin tirakkuṅkāṉ mukaṉuntāṅ-koṇṭatu koṭukkuṅkāṉ mukaṉumvē ṟākutal-paṇṭu mivvulakat tiyaṟki (22), irāvaṇaṉ maliyi eṭuttal(38), nūṟṟuvar talivaṉik kuṟaṅkaṟuttal (52), mallari maṟañcāytta māl (134) kuṅkiliya meḻutiṉa oḷivirinta toḻilkaḷi yuṭiyavākiya vāykaḷiyuṭiya marattāṟ ceyta ciṟupāviyālum pāṉiyālum ciṟupeṇkaḷ viḷiyāṭutal (59), kokkurittāṟ pōṉṟa maṭuppiyuṭiyavaṉ (94), maraṅkaḷiṟṟeyva muṟital(101), tuccātaṉaṉi vīmaṉ koṉṟatu (101) cikaṇṭi turōṇaṉik kollal (101), āycciyar taṅ kaṇavar kikaḷik kōttuk kuravi yāṭutal (106) cuṟāviṉatu maruppāṟ ceyta palakiyik kōttup puṟavitaḻoṭinta neytaṟ pūvi neṭiya nārilē toṭuttu aḻaku peṟak kaṭṭik kiyālē vācittaliyuṭiya yāḻ(131).
6. cintāmaṇiyiṟ kāṇappaṭum paḻiya ceytikaḷ:
aruntati kāṭṭal (2669), āviyaṉṉa tukil (67), elimayirp pōrvi (2680), ēṉāti mōtirañ ceṟitta cēṉātipati (2167), kallūri (395), kutirikaḷiṉ tuṭikaḷil avi piṟanta iṭappeyari eḻututal (2215), yāṉiyaraciṉ valamarup pīrntu ceyta cīppu (2436), muḻantāḷaḷa vāka vīraruṭukku muṭi (468), valampuri vaṭivac ceppu (2475), poṉ ceynāṇiṟ kuñciyik kaṭṭutal (2288).
7. paripāṭalil kāṇappaṭuñ ceytikaḷ:
intiraṉ cāpamēṟṟatu (19) immiyiṉ paṅ kuṟittuk kaṉṉiyar tinnīrāṭal (11), irākukētukaḷ kāṇappaṭuvaṉa valla veṉpatu (12), iḷiya makaḷiruṭiya aḻuki tīrttaṟkut tāyar puli puli yeṉṟu accuṟuttal (14), nakattilum kaṉṉattilum cempañcu eḻututal (6), paḻantēṉāṟ ceyta tēṟal(16), puṇṇiya natiyil nīrāṭuvōr poṉ mutaliyavaṟṟāl ceyvitta mīṉ mutaliyavaṟṟi atil cellaviṭutal(16), poṉṉāṟ ceyta naṇṭu iṟavu vāḷikaḷi āṟṟil viṭutal (10), makaḷir tēri makaḷir ōṭṭutal (11), makaḷir piṭiyi ūrtal (10), parattiyar māvum piṭiyum ūrtal (10).
8. peruṅkatiyil kāṇappaṭum ceytikaḷ:
pacu vaḻipāṭu (1-34-16), piraccōtaṉaṉukkut tēviyar patiṉāṟā yiravar (1-34-77), cītēvi yeṉṉum talikkōlam (1-34-121), poṉṉā lākiya ciṟupāttiram (1-34-160), talikkōr peṇṭir (1-35-72), pālāvi pōṉṟa nuṇṇiya āṭi (1-36-64), vipūti (1-36-225), poṟpūṇ kaṭṭiya pirampu (1-37-9), pēy piṭittāṭuvōriṉ piṉ paṟikkoṭṭic cellal (1-37-246), kaṭikiyāram (1-38-123), poṟiyoṟṟu (muttiri) (1-38-286) māṉkaṇ pōṉṟa cāḷaram (1-40-9), eṭṭippaṭṭam peṟṟa aracakumaraṉ (1-40-116), koṭivaṭiva meḻutiṉa mārpu (1-40-143), mulikkaccu (1-40-144), pañcakuviyam(1-40-266), aracaṉukkup piḻiceytōr tammuṭiya aḷavuḷḷa poṟpāviyoṉṟic ceyvittu avaṉ ciṉattip pōkkutal (1-40-371,4) neṟkatiri makaḷir cūṭutal (1-42-68), kāvitip paṭṭattuk kaṟikuṟi yākak koṭukkappaṭum poṟpaṭṭam (1-42-174), kayal vaṭivākac ceyta veṟṟilippi (1-44-229), pulivaṭivam aminta kāliyuṭiya kaṭṭil (1-47-175) elimayirāṟ ceyyappaṭṭa pūttoḻili yuṭiya pōrvi (1-47-179) teṉṉaṅkaḷḷum tēṉāṟ camitta kaḷḷiṉ teḷivum (2-2-177), aracaṉ ciṅkācaṉattil vīṟṟiruntu muṭipuṉiyu miṭattup paṭṭattut tēviyum uṭaṉiruttal (2-4-24), aracili pōl poṟṟakaṭṭāṟ ceyyappaṭṭa āparaṇam (2-7-73), maṇikaḷ iḻitta mōtiram (2-12-74), taṉpeyareḻutiṉa mōtiram (3-9-70), pavaḻattāṟ ceyta kumiḻiyuṭiya poṉṉālākiya mitiyaṭi (3-22-202), cittiraṅkaḷ eḻutappaṭṭa uyarnta māṭaṅkaḷ (4-3-35), kaṇavaṉip pirintuṟiyum makaḷir kūntal puṉiyāmi (4-7-103), yāṉittantattāṟ ceyta veṇpūkkōvikaḷ (4-14-64), pulimuka māṭam (4-15-108).
9. aiṅkuṟunūṟṟalaṟiyappaṭuvaṉa:
iṭiyar karumpu kuṇilāk kaṉiyukuttal, iḷamakaḷir tumpi māliyaṇital, poymmippuli kāvalukku vikkappaṭutal, kuraṅku mūṅkililēṟi viḷiyāṭutal, curañcelvōr palaviṉ kaṉiyi aruntic cellal, parattiyit tērēṟṟi varutal, parattiyarōṭu talimakaṉ puṉalāṭal, makaḷir iṭiyil poṟkācaṇital, makaḷir pantāṭutal, makaḷir eri pukutal, mutali taṉ piḷḷiyit tiṉṉutal.
10. cilappatikārattilaṟiyappaṭuvaṉa:
antiyil makaḷir malariyum aṟukiyum nelliyum maṅkalamākac cintutal, āṉimēṟ paṟiyaṟital, iraṇṭāñ cāmattiṟa kaṇṭa kaṉā eṭṭumātattiṟ palikkumeṉpatu, tēvācurayuttam patiṉeṭṭāṇṭilum, irāma irāvaṇa yuttam patiṉeṭṭu mātattilum muṭintateṉpatu. iṟantavarkaḷi uttēcittut tāṉañ ceytal, iṟivaṉ cūṭiya piṟi iraṇṭu kalittu eṉpatu, uyirppaliyuṇṇum mayirkkaṇ muracu, eṇṇeṇkaliyōr (vēciyar), eḻunili māṭam kaṭiyil iṉṉa carakku īṇṭuḷḷateṉṟu aṟivikkak koṭikaḷeṭuttal, kalaṅkari viḷakkam, kaṟpuṭiya makaḷir irukkum nāṭṭil maḻi peyyumeṉpatu, cañcīvi nāṉku vaki -calliya karaṇi, cantāṉa karaṇi, camaṉiya karaṇi, miruta cañcīvi; cantiraṉ kōyil, cūriyaṉ kōyil, kaḷavu nūl, teyvattip pūvum pukiyum cāntum kaṇṇiyum koṇṭu vaḻipaṭutal, nūlēṇi, pulippallit tāliyākak kaṭṭutal, potikaḷiṉ mēl aḷavu, eṇ, niṟi poṟikkappaṭṭiruttal, yāṉip piṭariyil makaḷiri yēṟṟi nakarkku maṇattiyaṟivittal.
27. maturi
(2000 varuṭaṅkaḷukku muṉ)
“tiru vaḻutināṭeṉṉuñ ciṟappup peyar peṟṟa pāṇṭi maṇṭalam kuṟiñci, mulli, marutam, neytal, pāli eṉap periyōrāl variyaṟukkappaṭṭa aivaki nilaṅkaḷum porunti iṉitu viḷaṅkum. kuṟiñci nilattilē iñciyum mañcaḷum miḷakum veṇciṟukaṭukum tōri nellum aivaṉa nellum vekuvāyp palkiyirukkum. kuṟattiyar tiṉikkiḷi kaṭiyum ōciyum kāṉavar avari mēyum āmāvik kaṭikiṉṟa pūcalum, pūkkoyyum makaḷir puli, puli yeṉṟu kūṟumoliyum māṟimāṟi oliyā niṟkum. mulliyiṉkaṇ eḷḷiḷaṅkāyum varakiṉ katirum orupāl muṟṟiyirukkum. orupāl ciṟutiṉi koyvar. aṅku miṅkumāḻnta kuḻikaḷil irattiṉaṅkaḷ kiṭantu viḷaṅkum. paciya payirkaḷiṉ iṭaṅkaṭōṟum mucuṇṭippūvum mullippūvum utirntu kiṭakkum. marutanilattil kaṉikaṭōṟum yāṉi niṉṟāl maṟiyum paṭiyāka neṟpayir paccippacēleṉa vaḷarntirukkum. taṭākaṅkaḷil tāmariyum nīlōṟpalamum cētāmpalum malarntu naṟumaṇam vīcum. kaḻaṉikaḷilum kuḷaṅkaḷilum maṭukkaḷilum valiñar mīṉkaḷip piṭittu avikaḷik koṉṟu kuvikkum āravāramum, karumpiṟkiṭṭa āli yiṭattōciyum, kaḷi paṟippiṭattōciyum, nellarikiṉṟa kiṇi yiṉ oliyum, āṇkaḷum peṇkaḷum nīrāṭukiṉṟa iriccalum ākāyattē ceṉṟu muḻaṅkum. neytalākiya kāṉaliṉiṭattē muttum vaḷiyum paratar tanta palvakip paṇṭaṅkaḷum vili ceyyappaṭum. yavaṉa mutaliya tēyattiṉar kutiri mutaliyavaṟṟik koṇarntu koṭuttu, veḷḷuppum tīmpuḷiyum, koḻutta mīṉkaḷit tuṇittu uppiṭṭularttiya karuvāṭukaḷum eṉṟivi pōṉṟavaṟṟik koṇṭu pōvar. kuṟiñcikkum mullikkum iṭiyilēyuḷḷa pāli nilattil, āṟali kaḷvar vaḻippōvāri varuttā vaṇṇam aracaṉāl ēvappaṭṭa vīrar, ili vēynta kuṭilil māṉṟōlākiya paṭukki yuṭiyarāyp poruntiyiruppar.
“ivvintiṇip pāṅkum aḻakuṟappeṟṟup pāṭalkaḷ cāṉṟa naṉṉāṭṭu naṭuvil, tiruvālavāy, tirunaḷḷāṟu, tirumuṭaṅki, tirunaṭuvūr eṉṉum nāṉmāṭak kūṭal eṉap peyar peṟṟa maturimā nakaramāṉatu pūmātiṉ aḻakiya mukampōl polivuṟṟirukkum. innakaric cūḻn tirukkum matil kaṟpaṭikaḷ palavuḷavāy viṇṇuṟa ōṅka, ataṉic cūḻnta akaḻiyāṉatu nīlamaṇi pōlum nīriyuṭittāy maṇṇuṟa āḻntirukkum. neṭiya niliyiṉiyum tiṇṇiya kataviṉiyumuṭiya vāyiliṉ mītamarnta māṭamāṉatu mēkamulāvum malipōl ōṅki yirukkum. māntarum māvum iṭiyaṟāmal vaḻaṅkutalāl vāyilāṉatu iṭiviṭātōṭukiṉṟa viyiyāṟṟiṉip pōṉṟirukkum. vāyilik kaṭanta māttirattil, akaṉṟa terukkaḷum avaṟṟiṉ irupuṟattilum cāḷaraṅkaḷ palavamintaṉavāy uyarntu tōṉṟum vīṭukaḷum, āṟum ataṉ irukariyum pōla viḷaṅkum.
“aṅkāṭit teruvilē, kōyilkaḷukku viḻākkaḷi naṭattik kaṭṭiṉa aḻakiya koṭikaḷum, taṇṭattalivar peṟṟa veṟṟiyiṉ poruṭṭu eṭutta camayakkoṭikaḷum kaṭkaṭikaḷil kaḷḷiṉ kaḷippu mikutiyic cāṟṟukiṉṟa koṭikaḷum iṉṉum palavāy vēṟupaṭṭa koṭikaḷum periya maliyiṭattu aruvikḷa aciyumāṟupōla aciyā niṟkum yāṉiyun tērum puraviyum vīrarum ākiya nāṟperum paṭikaḷ palkālum varuvaṉavāy mīḷkiyiṉālē, pū viṟpārum pūmāli viṟpārum cuṇṇam viṟpārum veṟṟiliyum pākkum cuṇṇāmpuṭaṉē viṟkiṉṟavarkaḷum tam iṉṉuyirañci ēṅkiyiruntu, annāṟpaṭiyum akaṉṟa piṉṉar varutta nīṅki., māṭamāḷiki kūṭa kōpuraṅkaḷiṉ kuḷir niḻalilē iruntu iḷippāṟuvārkaḷ. kiḻap peṇṭir paṇṇiyaṅkaḷi naṟumpūvuṭaṉē ēntic ceṉṟu maṉika ṭōṟum maṉikaṭōṟum ulāvi viṟpar.
“mālip poḻutil viḻāk kāṇac celvarkaḷ, paṭṭuṭuttu uṭivāḷ tarittu māli tāṅkit tērkaḷiṉ mītēṟik kālāṭkaḷ puṭi cūḻntuvarak kutirikaḷic celuttic celvar. at tiruviḻākkaḷip peṇkaḷ aramiyattalattiṉiṉṟuṅ kāṇpar. aṅkē kaṭṭiya koṭi kaḷiṉ mītu mantamārutam vīcutalāl ammakaḷir mukaṅkaḷāṉavi mēkat tilē maṟiyuñ cantiraṉip pōla orukāṟṟōṉṟi orukāl maṟiyum.
“putta matattiṉarākiya pēriḷampeṇṭir ciṟupiḷḷikaḷi eṭuttukkoṇṭu, taṅkaṇavarōṭu pūcikku vēṇṭum pūviṉiyum tūpaṅkaḷiyum kikaḷil ēntic ceṉṟu vaṇaṅkum puttap paḷḷikaḷ ōriṭattuk kāṇappaṭum. ciṟanta vētaṅkaḷi ōti, yākāti karumaṅkaḷic ceytu, piraman tāṅkaḷēyāy, vīṭṭiṉ patti ivvulakilē tāṉē aṭikiṉṟa piramavittukkaḷiruppiṭam orupāl pulappaṭum. orupāl, mukkāla oḻukkamum mūvulakiṉ ceytiyum muḻutuṇarum camaṇa muṉivarkaḷ uṟikiṉṟa amaṇap paḷḷikaḷ kaṭpulaṉām. appāl aṟaṅ kūṟaviyamum, amiccarirukkiyum aḻakuṟa viḷaṅkum.
“caṅkuvaḷi kaṭivārum, maṇikaḷit tuḷiyiṭuvārum, poṟ kollarum; poṉvāṇikarum, āṭiviṟpārum, cittirakkārarum piṟaruṅ kūṭi antikkaṭiyil neruṅki niṟpar. aṟakkūḻccāliyil palāppaḻam vāḻippaḻam muntirikippaḻam mutaliya paḻaṅkaḷiyum, pākaṟkāy vāḻikkāy vaḻutuṇaṅkāy mutalāṉa kāykaḷiyum, ili kaṟikaḷiyum, kaṇṭa carukkarit tēṟṟiyum, iṟiccikaḷiyuṭiya cōṟṟiyum kiḻaṅkukaḷuṭaṉē pāṟcōṟu pāl mutaliyaṉavaṟṟiyum koṇṭuvantu paci tākaṅkaḷiṉāl varuntuvōrkkum tarittirarkkum iṭuvārkaḷ. ikkūṟiya palaviṭaṅkaḷilum eḻukiṉṟa ōciyāṉatu antikkālattil palvēṟu paṟavikaḷ kūṭiya iṭattu eḻukiṉṟa ōciyiṉ taṉmiyatākum.
“poḻutu cāyntapiṉ āṭalpāṭal mutaliya palvakit tuṟi kaḷil avvavar poḻutik kaḻippar. mutal yāmañ ceṉṟapiṉ kaṭi viṟpār kaṭikaḷiyaṭittum, paṇṇiyam viṟpār avaṟṟiṉ pāṅkarum paṭuttuṟaṅkat. tirunāḷiṉkaṇṇē kūttāṭuṅ kūttar aḵtoḻintu tuyil koḷḷa, oli niṟintaṭaṅkiṉa kaṭaliyokkap paṭukkiyilē tuyil koḷvōr iṉitākak kaṇvaḷarvar.. piṉpu vikaṟiyāmattil, antaṇar vētam ōtuvar. yāḻōr narampi iṉitākat teṟittu marutatti vācippar. paṇṭam viṟpār tam kaṭikaḷi meḻukutalic ceyvar. kaṟpuṭiya makaḷir tuyiluṇarnteḻuntu,
“kāṭṭuk kaḷintu kalaṅkaḻīi yillatti
yāppinī reṅkun teḷittuc ciṟukāli
nīrccāl karaka niṟiya malaraṇintu
illampoliya vaṭuppiṉuḷ tīppeyka”
eṉṟapaṭi akkālattu illattiṟ ceyyat takuvaṉavaṟṟic ceyyāniṟpar. kōḻic cēvalkaḷ viṭiyaṟkālatti aṟintu kūvā niṟkum. cēvaṟ paṟavikaḷ tattam pēṭikaḷi aḻiyā niṉṟu tīṅkural ceyyum. kūṭṭilē uṟikiṉṟa karaṭi puli mutaliya valiya vilaṅkukaḷ muḻaṅkāniṟkum. mutuku kāṭṭip pōnta vīrarum veṭṭuṇṭa vīrarum viṭuttayāṉikaḷum kutirikaḷum āṉirikaḷum, tōlvi yaṭinta aracarkaḷ tiṟamiyākak koṇarnta kalaṅkaḷum, avi pōṉṟaṉa piṟavum araṇmaṉiyi nōkkic cellum. iṅṅaṉam kaṅkiyāṟu kaṭaliṟ kalantāṟ pōla, ulakiṟ poruḷkaḷellām maturiyi aṭiyum.
“im mānakariṉ kōyilkoṇṭu ceṅkōl celuttum pāṇṭiyaṉ ‘vikaṟiyāman tuyileḻuntu kālik kaṭaṉik kaḻittuc cantaṉam pūci, mārpilē mālitāṅkik kiyilē vīravaḷiyuṭaṉ kaṇiyāḻiyic ceṟittuk, kañciyiṭṭa tukiliyuṭuttu, ataṉmē laṇiyum aṇikalaṉkaḷi aḻakuṟavaṇintu, kolumaṇṭapattilē vīṟṟiruppāṉ. irunta vaḷavilē, ēṉātippaṭṭa mutaliya ciṟappup peṟṟa paṭittalivar avaṉatu veṟṟit tiṟatti vāḻttuvar. piṉṉar aracaṉ tāṉ nāṭunakar peṟumpaṭiyākavum, taṉ veṟṟi ciṟakkumpaṭiyākavum utaviya vīrariyellāmaḻittup pāvalaruṭaṉē pāṇar pāṭṭi yariyum aḻittu yāṉi tēr eṉpaṉa pōṉṟa paricaḷippāṉ. aṅṅaṉam pala pala poruḷkaḷik koṇarntu koṭuttalāl an nāṭṭil vāḻuṅkuṭimakkaḷ celvam perukiyiruppar.”(maturikkāñci)
“1800 varuṭaṅkaḷukku muṟpaṭṭa tamiḻar” eṉṉum nūliṟ kāṇappaṭum kavaṉittaṟkuriya cila poruḷkaḷ.

“rōmar ki.pi.226 ilum muciṟiyil 840 mutal 1200 vīrariyuṭiya paṭi oṉṟu vittiruntārkaḷeṉṟum, avviṭattil ākasṭacukku oru kōvil kaṭṭappaṭṭiruntatākavum ‘Malabar Manual ’ eṉṉum nūl kūṟuva tākak kaṉakacapip piḷḷi avarkaḷ “1800 varuṭaṅkaḷukku muṟpaṭṭa tamiḻar” eṉṉum nūlil kāṭṭiyuḷḷār. (pa. 38)
nākariṭamiruntē āriyar eḻutteḻutum muṟiyik kaṟṟārkaḷ. ākavē, avarkaḷiṉ eḻuttut tēvanākari eṉappaṭukiṉṟatu.-1800 āṇṭukaḷukku muṟpaṭṭa tamiḻar, (tēvanākari (tēvar nakariṟ pēcappaṭuvatu) eṉpatē tēvanākari yāyiṟṟu eṉak kūṟappaṭukiṉṟatu.)
cumattirāvilē laputōvā eṉṉumiṭattil oru cācaṉam kiṭittatu. atil caka varuṭam 1010 kuṟippiṭappaṭṭirukkiṉṟatu. atu kuṟitta āṇṭu 1088kkuc cari. accācaṉam āyirattivar eṉṉum kūṭṭat tiṉar koṭutta naṉkoṭiyippaṟṟik kūṟukiṉṟatu. ic cācaṉattāl cumattirāvil ki.pi. 11ām nūṟṟāṇṭil potumakkaḷiṉ cācaṉaṅkaḷil tamiḻ vaḻaṅkiṟṟu eṉpatu veḷiyākiṉṟatu. nām nampak kūṭāta avvaḷavu tūrattil tamiḻatum tamiḻaratumāṉa celvākku iruntirukkiṉṟatu.1
rāypakatūr. veṅkiyā em.ē,. araciṉar cācaṉap paricōtakar.
2. pāṇṭiya maṉṉar

neṭuñceḻiyaṉ (ki.pi.75-90)
(āriyappaṭi veṉṟavaṉ, ciṅkācaṉattiṟ ṟuñciyavaṉ)
veṟṟivēṟceḻiyaṉ (ki.pi.75-90)

"The puranas relate that Manu, the Lord of the Dravidas divided the country into two halves giving southern portion to his son Yama and the northern where his capital was situated to his beloved dughter Ela. To this day the southern land now submerged under the sea is known to the masses as the dominion ofYama, the Lord of the South. The land of Ela was Kumari nadu ruled and presided by queens. Even today in Malabar, a portion of the Tamilagam, the queen is the owner of the kingdom and the brother who governs the people is her representative. The succession descends in the female line; and women in Malabar are heiresses and owners of property and are treated with greater regard and consideration than men.- Tamilian Antiquary No.1.

2. "Geology and Natural History make it alike certin at a time within the bounds of human knowledge, Southern India did not form part of Asia. 3. India, South Africa and Australia were connected by an Indo oceanic continent in the Permian Epoch." -H.F.Blanford
2. "Peninsular India or the Deccan (literally, the country to the South) is geologically distinct from the Indo-Gangetic Plin and the Himalaya. It is the remins of a former continent which stretched continuously to Africa in the space now, occupied by the Indian Ocean. The rocks of which it is formed are among the Oldest in the world and show no traces of having ever been submerged. In many parts they are overlid by a sheet of black 'trap' rock or basalt, which flowed over them as molten lava. In the Deccan we are therefore, in the first days of the world. we see land substantially as it existed before the beginning of life. The Indo Gangetic plin stretches without a break from Indus on the west to the Delta of the Ganges on the east, a distance of twleve hundred miles. When the world was still in the making and before the elevation of the Himalaya, the space now occupied by this plin was a sea, the southern shore of this sea was what is peninsular India. With the rise of the Himalaya the sea disappeared and the rivers drining the Himalaya flowed, into the depression bringing with them the silt which is now the soil of the plin.
-People and Problems of India P.23,24- Sir.T.W. Holderness, G.C.B.

1. "The Indian Ocean formed a continent which extended from Sunda Islands along the coast of Asia to the east coast of Africa. This large continent is of great importance from being the possible cradle of the human race."
"There are a number of circumstances (Specially chronological facts) which suggest that the primeval home of man was a continent now sunk below the surface of the Indian Ocean which extended along the South of Asia as it is at present (and probably in direct connection with it) towards the east as far as further India and the Sunda Islands, towards the west as far as the Madagascar and the south shores of Africa.
"This large continent of former times Sclatter, an English man has called Lemuria from the monkey like animals which inhabited it and it is at the same time of great importance from being the probable cradle of the human race which in all likelihood here first developed out of anthropoid apes."-Prof. Ernest Haeckel.
"The locality of the origin of the earliest race from recent researches appears to have been on lands now submerged beneath the Indian Ocean-Science of man Australia, Dec.1900."

"Investigations in relation to race show it is by no means impossible that Southern India was once the passage ground by which the ancient progenitors of northern and mediterranean races proceeded to the parts of the globe which they now inhabit. Human remins and traces have been found on the east coast of an age which is indeterminate but quite beyond the ordinary calculation of history. Antiquarian research is only now beginning to find means of supplementing the deficiency cused by the absence of materials constructed or collected by usual historical methods. These results are specially to be regarded as without doubt, the people who have for many ages occupied this portion of the peninsula are a people influencing the whole world not perhaps be moral and intellectual attributes to a great extent by superior physical qualities." - Dr. E. Maclean.
1. Their (The Dravidians) settlements in India extent to such prehistoric times that we cannot even feel sure that we regard them as immigrants or, at least, as either conquerors or colonists on a large scale but rather as aboriginal in the sence in which that term is usually understood.
The hypothesis, that would represent what we know of their history most correctly, would place their original seat in the extreme south, somewhere probably not far from Madura or Tanjore and thence spreading fan like towards the north. They have no traditions which point to any seat of race out of India, or of their having migrated from any country whose inhabitants they can clim any kindred so far as they know they are indigenous and aboriginal.-Dr.Fergusson quoted in Tamilian Antiquary No.1

1. The marvellous uniformity of the flood legends of all parts of the globe, alone remins to be dealt with. Whether these are some varctic version of the story of the lost Atlantis and the submergence or whether they are echoes of a great cosmic parable once tught and held in reverence in some common centre whence they have reverberated throughout the world, does not immediately concern us. Sufficient for our purpose it is to show the universal acceptation of these legends.
"It would be needless waste of time and space to go over these flood stories one by one, suffice to say, that in India, Chaldea, Babylon Media, Greece, Scandinavia, China among the Jews and the Keltic tribes of Britin the legend is absolutely identical in all essentials. Now turn to the west and what do we find? The same story presented in its every detil-presented among the Mexicans (each tribe having in its version) the people of Guatemala, Honduras, Peru and amongst every tribe of North American Indians; It is puerile to suggest that mere coincidence can account for this fundamental identity - The story of Atlantis - W.Scott Elliot p.16.

1. The enthnic type of Sumerians, so strongly masked in their statures and reliefs, was so different from those of the races which surrounded them, as was their language from those of the Semitics, Aryans or others. The face type of the average Indian of today is no doubt much the same as that of his Dravidian race ancestors thousands of years ago. Among the modern Indians, as amongst the modern Greeks or Italians the ancient pre-Aryan type of the land has (as the primitive of the land always does) survived, while that of the Aryan conqueror died out long ago and it is to this Dravidian ethinic type of India that the Sumerians bear most resemblance, so far as we can judge from his monuments. He was very like a southern Hindu of the Dekkan (who still speaks Dravidian languages) and it is by no means impossible that the Sumerians were an Indian race which passed certinly by land, perhaps also by sea through Persia to the valley of the two rivers. It was in the Indian Home (perhaps the Indus Valley that their writing may have been invented and progressed from a purely pictorial to a simplified and abbreviated from which after wards in Babylonia took on it peculiar uniform appearance owing to its being written within a square ended stilus on soft clay. On the way they left the seeds of culture in Elam.. there is little doubt that India must have been one of the earliest centres of civilization and it seems natural to suppose that the strange unsemitic, un-Aryan people who came from the east to civilise the west were of Indian origin, specially when we see with our eyes how very Indian the sumerians were in type. "F.N." The civilization was not Aryan. The culture of India is pre-Aryan in origin as in Greece the conquered civilized the conquerors. The Aryan in India owed his civilization and his degeneration to the Dravidians as the Aryan Greek did to Myceneans - Dr.Hall.

3. The Madras Presidency is the habitant of a Tamil Race whose civilization was the most ancient. A branch of Tamil Race spread a vast civilization on the Euphrates in very ancient times whose Astrology, Religion, Love, Morals and Rites etc., furnished the foundation of the Ariya and Babylonian civilization and whose mythology was the source of the Christian Bible. Another branch of them spread from the Malabar coast and gave rise to a wounderful Egyptian civilization and the Aryans are indebted to the race in many aspects. The colossal temples in South India proclim the Engineering Art of the Tamil Race-Quoted in Prabuddha Bharatha. Sep.1921.

1. 'Telugu is one of the great Dravidian languages. The word is probably derived from Trilinga (the threelingas of Siva) a name for the old Kingdom of Andhra. It was at one time called by Europeans 'Gentoo' from a Portuguese word meaning gentle. The Telugu-speaking people are partly subjects of Nizam of Hyderabad and partly under British rule beginning north of Madras city, extending north-west to Bellary where Telugu meets Kanarese and north-east to near Orissa. They are taller and fairer than Tamils, otherwise they are of typical Dravidian features. They are of an enterprising people, good farmers and skillful seamen...In 1901 the number of speakers in Telugu in India was nearly 21millions. - Encyclopedea Britannica.
1. Malayalam a language of Dravidian family spoken on the west coast of Southern India. It is believed to have developed out of Tamil as recently as the 9th century. It possesses a large literature in which words borrowed from Sanskrit are conspicuous. In 1901 the total number of speakers of Malayalam in all India was just about six millions.- Encyclopedia Britannica.
1. "Kanarese, language of Dravidian family spoken by about ten millions of people in South India chiefly in Mysore, Hyderabad and the adjoining districts of Madras and Bombay. It has an ancient literature written in an alphabet closely resembling that employed for Telugu. Since the 12th Century the Canarese speaking people have largely adopted the Lingayat form of faith which may be described as an anti-Brahmanical sect of Siva worshippers; most of them are agriculturists but they also engage actively in trade.-Encyclopedea Britannica.
2. "Telugu does not take its avilable literature much anterior to the eleventh centuryA.D. and this literature seems to be modelled upon Sanskrit entirely. Kanarese has certinly a more ancient literature. A work of the 9th century undoubtedly is the Kaviraja Marga of Nirpatungu. Malayalam seems to have grown out of Tamil in the early centuries of the Christian era." - Prof. S.Krishnaswami Aiyangar.
3. Tulu or Tuluva, a language of the Dravidian family,found chiefly in the South Kanara District of Madras. It has no literature, nor it has been adopted for
official use even where it is spoken by the majority of the population. In 1901 the total number of speakers of Tulu exceeded half a million."-Encyclopedea Britannica.
1. Singhalese is essentially, Dravidian language. Its evolution too seems to have been on a Tamil basis and so we seem safe in saying that, while in regard to its word development. Singhalese is the child of Pali and Sanskrit: it is with regard to its physical features and structure essentially the dugthter of Tamil. with regard to the laws governing the relation of words in a sentence, viz. the laws of syntax, including idioms we find great many laws which cannot be explined except in the principle of Tamil grammar. - Mudaliar W.F.Gunawardhana

1. “Dialects of the same family of languages were spoken throughout India, except in the Vindhyan regions in the Neolithic Age: and that is what has been called the Dravidian family. The distinction between the spoken dialects of North India, to which the name Gaudian has been given by modren scholars and which have been held to be degenerations of Sanskrit or of Prakrit, and those of Southern India, to which the name Dravidian has been given, is I hold , a distinction without a difference, except that the North Indian dialects have been very much more profoundly affected by Sanskrit than those of South India. The Neolithians of North India spoke languages of their own which, I hold, were structurally allied to the so called Dravidian family of languages and not to Sanskrit or Prakrit" (Stone age in India, p.43.)

1. The vedic culture which resembles that of the Homeric Greeks or the Celtic Irish at the beginning of the Christian era, or that of the pre-Christian Teutons and slaves becomes transformed in the epics into the Hindu culture through the influence of Dravidians. The Aryan idea of worship during the earliest period was to call on the farther sky or some other shining one to look from on high on the sacrificer, and receive from him the offerings of fat or flesh: cakes and drink. But soon Puja or worship takes the place of Homa or sacrifice. Image worship which was a striking feature of the Dravidian faith was accepted by the Aryans. The ideals of vegetarianism and non violence (Ahimsa) also developed. The Vedic tradition has dominated by the Agamic and today Hindu culture shows the influence of the Agama as much as that of the Vedas. The Aryan and the Dravidian do not exist side by side in Hinduism " Contact with the highly civilized Dravidians led to the transformation of Vedism into a theistic religion." (Sir Radhakrishnan)
2. "It need not be thought that the Aryan was always the superior force. There are occasions when the Aryan yielded to the non Aryan and rightly too the epic relates the manner in which the different non-Aryan ones Krishna's struggle with Indra, the prince of the Vedic gods is one instance. The rise of the cult of Siva is another." (Sir Radhakrishnan)

1. The Hymns of Rig Veda- The hymns which are collected in this work are 1028 in number and were composed during several centuries. They are only the mate-rials we have for the history of this early period, which is called the Vedic Age.
“The people of India recognised four Vedas, and this is how they have grown up, some of the hymns by an ancient custom chanted at sacrifices instead of being recited and a separate collection was made of these chanted hymns and called Sama Veda. Special sacrificial formulas and rules also existed from ancient times for the performance of rites, and these formulas and rules were collected under the form of Yajur Veda and a collection of the latter hymns often consisting of charms and incantations aginst evil influences grew up under the name of Atharva Veda." - The civilization of India by Romesh C.Dutt, C.I.E.

1. vēṭṭiyāṭuvōr mirukaṅkaḷiyō paṟavikaḷiyō taṅkaḷ toḻiṟkicintu naṭakkumāṟu paḻakki vali mutaliyaṉa kaṭṭumiṭattil viṭuvārkaḷ. ivaṟṟāl nampikki koṇṭu ivaṟṟiṉ iṉaṅkaḷ vantu kaṇṇiyil māṭṭikkoḷḷum. ivaṟṟuḷ paṭciyip pārvip paṭciyeṉṟum, mirukattip pārvi mirukameṉṟuṅ kūṟutal marapu.
1. ivar intiyāvukku vantu kapilariṉ cāṅkiya nūṟkoḷkikaḷik kaṟṟu avikaḷik kirēkka nāṭṭiṟku paravac ceytār eṉappaṭukiṉṟaṉar.
1. Pon, a gold coin valued at Rs.2.50 each (Tamil Studies P.260)
1. "Thiruvalluvamali or the garland of Thiruvalluvar like every other account relating to this famous moralist is a strange mixture of doubtful traditions and absurd fictions written by some later Dravidian author of the 9th century to popularize the celebrated work to Thiruvalluvar." (Tamil Studies .p. 247)
1. "Ancient Tamil literature and the Greek and Roman authors prove that in the first two centuries of the Christin era the ports of the Coromandal or Chola Coast enjoyed the benefits of active commerce with both the West and the East. The Chola fleets did not confine themselves to coasting Voyages. but boldly crossed to Bay of Bengal to the mouths of the Ganges and Irrawaddy and the Indian ocean to the Islands of Malay Archipelago." (Vincent Smith)
1. Tamil Studies
2. The existence of pure Tamil words like eḻuttu, cuvaṭi etc., before they (Aryans) came to south disproves the theory Agastya brought the alphebet with him from Upper India. (P.122)
"They had and still have their own terms pertining to agriculture, anatomy architecture, astronomy, commerce, domestic economy , family relations , fauna and flora, language and literature, medicine, minerals, politics, religion, war, weight and measures, etc, all of course in their primitive stage. nāṟu,cey, ñāyiṟu, tiṅkaḷ, ki, kāl, māṟukōḷ, nel, pāl, muṟṟam, maccu, tāy, appaṉ, teṟku, tāḻi, vali, pūci, eḻuttu, col, pā, tiṇi, nō, vali, veḷḷi, poṉ, iṟi, ūr, kaṭavuḷ, ampu, vil, mā, kaḻañcu are all pure Tamil words.
"nāṭu kūṟṟam, kōṭṭam as administrative divisions of a country, nellāyam, kārttikip pacci, kiṉmi, taṟiyiṟi, cekkiṟi, makaṉmi etc,. as names of public taxes amāttiyam, vāriyam, kāvitimi, campiriti etc, as official terms, kuṟuṇi, patakku, tūṇi, muntiri, kāṇi, kaḻañcu and other words of native weight and measures... kācu, paṇam, tuṭṭu, varākaṉ and other enominations of old coinage.
1. "It was from the Nagas that the Aryas first learnt the art of writing; and hence Sanskrit characters are to this day known as Deva-nagari. (Tamils 1800 year ago. p.45)
"At Laboe Toewa, Paros, Sumatra has been found a Tamil inscription dated in the Saka year 1010-A.D. 1088. It records a gift by a body of persons who are styled the one-thousand five hundred. This epigraph proves that the Tamil language was used in public documents on the Island of Sumatra in the 11th Century A.D. accordingly, the influence which the Tamils and their language exercised is much greater than the traditional boundaries would warrant us to believe. - Rai Bahadur V.Venkayya, M.A.,
- Epigraphist to the Government of India.
